# 0160-flight-recorder — failure-mode enumeration over the recommended design (2026-08-23)

The **design-level** pass the research plan made a standing obligation (plan question 15:
"the report carries one standing obligation — re-run this enumeration over the recommended
design"). It is the second D-0036-shape pass on this track: the first ran over the *plan*,
and its register is [plan-fmea-enumeration.md](plan-fmea-enumeration.md). The object here is
the recommended design in [report.md](report.md) — the capture interface of Key question 6
with its invariants I1–I10, and every ruling in Key questions 1–16.

> **Read the `report.md:NNN` citations below as historical.** This register cites the report by
> line number about 199 times, and it was written against the report as it stood at commit
> `08d5bbe` — *before* the revision against intake items a–l and before the acceptance-gate
> corrections. The report has since grown by hundreds of lines, so most of those line numbers now
> land somewhere else, and a few point at text that was changed *because of the finding citing it*.
> The register is preserved verbatim, which is the point of a register; this note is the repair.
> Follow the quoted text and the `Q<n>`/`I<n>` labels, not the line numbers. (Added 2026-08-23 by
> the acceptance gate, which measured 34 of the 40 mechanically checkable `Q<n> … report.md:NNN`
> cites landing outside the question they name. A validator check for this was measured against the
> whole corpus and scored zero true hits, so it is a **do-not-build** under D-0037 and this
> convention is the substitute.)

## Method, and what it deliberately is not

Per **D-0036** this is the FMEA *enumeration* without the scoring apparatus: qualitative
severity only, no S/O/D, no RPN, no risk-priority arithmetic. It is a one-off pass over one
artifact, not a standing control — the same shape and the same limits as the plan-level pass.

Eleven agents, two stages:

- **Eight enumeration lanes** over the design's stages, each asked for concrete modes with a
  mechanism a reviewer can check, and each asked to map every mode to what in the design
  answers it — an interface invariant (`I<n>`), a report question (`Q<n>`), a named spike
  lane, `partial:<ref> — <gap>`, or `UNOWNED`. Lanes were told not to re-report modes the
  plan-level pass had already closed, and to report them anyway where the design's answer was
  inadequate or self-contradictory.
- **Three verification lanes** over the report itself, which the plan-level pass had no
  analogue for because there was no design to verify: internal consistency (prose against the
  interface artifact), claims against primary sources (the ten most load-bearing external
  claims, re-read at their pinned tags), and constraint/charter compliance (D-0040's five
  rulings, D-0001/D-0003/D-0004, the plan's sixteen questions, the D-0041 rubric).

Yield: **203 failure modes** across the eight lanes (55 unowned, 148
partially owned) and **53 verification findings**. Everything is preserved verbatim in
the appendices; the shortlist below is the adjudication.

**On the severity distribution**: the lanes rated 114 of 203 modes "high",
which is not a credible base rate and is not treated as one. Enumeration agents inflate
severity. The adjudication re-ranked by what would plausibly reach the field, and the register
keeps each lane's original rating rather than rewriting it, so a later reader sees the raw
signal rather than my filter of it.

## What this pass changed in the report

Twenty-eight amendments landed, each annotated in place in report.md with its date. The ones
that changed the design rather than its wording:

| # | Finding | Change |
|---|---|---|
| 1 | The interface ordered redaction *after* serialization while Q13 put it inside the walk — and key/path rules cannot run against a finished JSON string | Pipeline corrected to project → redact → serialize; the applier is the serializer's replacer |
| 2 | `RedactionRules.patterns` took live `RegExp` objects: a shared `/g` pattern carries `lastIndex`, so redaction skips alternate records — half the records ship the secret while the rest carry `{redacted}` markers, so the bundle looks scrubbed while leaking (and a frozen config flips it to throwing on every record instead, emptying every bucket) | Patterns are `{source, flags, ruleId}` data; the validator rejects `g`/`y`; the applier shares no mutable state |
| 3 | Redaction cost was the one capture-path step never budgeted — a backtracking pattern stalls the shared dispatch turn, and a stall is not a throw, so no counter sees it | Named as a hazard in Q11; bounded input; added to the spike list |
| 4 | Per-bucket sequences plus millisecond-clamped timestamps cannot order records *across* buckets — the interleaving that is the recorder's one distinct claim | Recorder-global `gseq` stamped alongside `seq` |
| 5 | I3 enforced an envelope the config surface had no field to express | `RecorderConfig.envelopeBytes`, one stated formula, shown in the worked example |
| 6 | Ring bytes are not wire bytes: every body is re-escaped inside the bundle's own JSON at roughly 1.4–1.7× | Stated in Q2; intake item c's ceiling question must be asked in wire bytes |
| 7 | `record()` re-entrancy: a getter that logs re-enters through the facade into the log bucket mid-eviction | Guard plus `drops.reentrant` |
| 8 | A later `window.onerror =` disarms the nets silently and permanently; taps had a full liveness apparatus and nets had none | `health().nets`, re-checked at every seal |
| 9 | The pre-arm stub is an inline head script and no CSP fact was ever elicited — the report used that same lens to disqualify fast-redact | Stated as a deployment dependency; new intake item m; external-file fallback |
| 10 | A bare `fetch` has no timeout, so one hung POST pins the delivery slot and its sealed bundle for the tab's life — the exact pinned-memory outcome bounded-then-drop exists to avoid | `AbortSignal.timeout` per attempt |
| 11 | No 429/`Retry-After` handling: one device-level cause makes a phase-aligned fleet-wide upload storm against an endpoint that is plausibly already failing | Server backpressure honoured — the half of Sentry's transport this report had not copied |
| 12 | 4xx retried like 5xx: a contract drift silences the fleet while the counter blames the network | 4xx drops immediately into `delivery.rejected` |
| 13 | The loss marker was a second wire format with no schema, no route, and no intake question — and a beacon's response is unobservable, so the client could never learn it was discarded | The marker joins the vendored contract with its own route and `formatVersion`; added to intake item c |
| 14 | `unknownBuckets`/`configErrors` were unbounded caller-keyed structures riding every header — one dynamic `attach()` name allocates forever inside the memory-bounded component | Both capped with overflow counters; keys redacted at seal |
| 15 | `crypto.randomUUID` is secure-context-only; on an http:// fleet it throws inside a constructor I8 forbids from throwing, disabling every recorder silently | Ids minted from `crypto.getRandomValues` |
| 16 | `createFlightRecorder` had no singleton guard — I3's envelope is per instance, and everyone reads it as per device | One recorder per page, enforced at construction |
| 17 | `detach()` was not epoch-guarded: the worked example's own dispose closure marked a *live* tap dead after a reconnect, producing the exact misdiagnosis the liveness block exists to prevent | Epoch-guarded detach; the closure is unregistered on recreate |
| 18 | "One observer per root actor" was unrepresentable against one-tap-per-bucket: N attaches would mark the bucket dead N−1 times or capture one machine of N | The adapter fans N roots into one tap and stamps `meta.sourceId` |
| 19 | Wire 1 has no gap concept, so a reconnect splices pre- and post-reconnect records into unbroken history with every counter at zero — while Q12 had pre-committed to gap markers | The adapter synthesizes a gap record from its wire-2 subscription |
| 20 | `FormData`/`Blob` bodies serialize to `{}` with no bound hit to detect — an upload recorded as an empty body, indistinguishable from a genuinely empty one | `cause: 'unserializable'` marker; binary bodies marked, never captured |
| 21 | The elision-marker mechanism could not observe what it must mark: the serializer sorts and *slices* keys before iterating, so breadth elisions never reach a replacer, and the array off-by-one emits nothing at all | Q3 rewritten — the wrapper owns the bound decisions, or the build re-prices the dependency; a spike question |
| 22 | `{elided}` carried no cause for circular references — the one elision the motivating shapes (the actor graph, reused Ajv errors) actually produce | `cause: 'circular'` added |
| 23 | `oldestIsComplete` could be true over a *truncated* checkpoint, promoting RD-5's anti-pattern from one record to a whole window | The oldest record must also be unmarked |
| 24 | The quarantine ride-along was promised in three sections, existed in no type, entered the bundle without passing the meter, and 0060's ring is count-bounded over exactly the traffic most likely to be huge | Typed as a byte-capped section filled by the transport adapter |
| 25 | Q9 replaced RD-7's config snapshot with a digest, but two redaction lists still named the snapshot as a target | Lists reconciled; the trade recorded in Q12 |
| 26 | Q8 specified a per-bucket window `{afterMs, afterCount}` that the design panel had explicitly rejected and the artifact does not carry | Q8 rewritten to the global `{ms, maxItems}` |
| 27 | `packetId` was promised on every MQTT record; wire 1 does not carry it (0070 constructs it inside the ingress this track refuses to tap) | Dropped from Q3/Q12; replay-time synthesis stated instead |
| 28 | The outbound-gap verdict leaned on an outbound-volume figure in no fact file and no assumption list — a D-0004 breach | Declared as an assumption, with a revisit trigger |

Smaller reconciliations also applied: the drop ledger went from four species to seven
(`recorded`, `tapErrors` and `reentrant` joined the original four) and I4's equation was bound
to fields a bundle actually carries; `creator` and `platform` were added to the header;
`supplements` became `baseBundleId`; Q11's `tapError` counter and self-protective detach were
given types and a note that the threshold governs tap protection only, never a trigger;
delivery counters were given a home in `health().delivery`; Q5's proposed abort counters were
routed through the transport adapter like `IngressStats`; `<RecorderErrorBoundary>` was
corrected to `createRecorderBoundary`; Q9's peak-memory figure gained its UTF-16 caveat; the
`oversized` species gained a validator rule that makes it defense-in-depth; intake item d was
cited in the assumptions; the Sentry breadcrumb mechanism was restated precisely (a
shallow-spread copy whose nested `data` stays live, not "the caller's object"); Bugsnag's
config fallback was de-"silent"ed (it logs a warning) and its citations marked branch-pinned
at 8.9.0; the attachment payload type was widened to `string | Uint8Array`; the keepalive
quota was scoped to the fetch group; the rubric's two evidence-asymmetric rows were flagged so
the verdict does not lean on them; and D-0006's inbound scope was stated.

## What was not acted on, and why

- **The 148 partially-owned modes** are, with few exceptions, the design working as
  intended: a `partial` verdict most often means the design answers the mode and leaves a
  spike-measurable quantity open — the per-record overhead constant, the projection budget on
  device-class hardware, the correlation open-set's cap. They are preserved in Appendix B and
  are required reading for whoever writes the spike, not defects to fix in prose.
- **Modes arguing for durable parking, cross-tab coordination, or recorder-owned remote
  configuration** were not acted on: each is a charter ruling (D-0040) or a design-panel
  refusal with its reasoning already recorded, and an enumeration lane is not the venue to
  reopen them. Where a lane's mechanism was genuinely new, it is noted against the existing
  refusal rather than used to overturn it.
- **Severity inflation was not corrected in place.** Modes rated "high" whose mechanism needs
  a configuration the validator rejects, or a platform behaviour this survey's own source
  reads contradict, stay in Appendix B unamended. The appendix records what was found, not a
  claim that all of it is true.
- **The report's residual unknowns** — the envelope's value between 10 and 50 MB, the bucket
  table, the endpoint's ceiling, the device class — are intake and spike work rather than
  amendable prose, and are named in the report's assumptions list.

## Appendix A — verification findings (verbatim)

### Internal consistency (report prose vs the interface artifact) (23)

**[high]** The design-level FMEA register the report says sits beside it does not exist — `tracks/0160-flight-recorder/design-fmea-enumeration.md` is absent from the working tree (report.md:563, echoed at :9, :27, :607).

- *Evidence*: report.md:563: "**The standing obligation is discharged beside this report**: the same D-0036-shape enumeration ... ran again over this report's recommended design; its register is [design-fmea-enumeration.md](design-fmea-enumeration.md)". `ls tracks/0160-flight-recorder/` returns only design-panel-record.md, plan-fmea-enumeration.md, report.md, research-plan.md; `git status` shows only report.md and design-panel-record.md untracked. The only enumeration in the tree is plan-fmea-enumeration.md (commit 94e7a08, "failure-mode enumeration over the plan"), which the report itself distinguishes from the design-level pass ("the same ... enumeration that amended the plan ran again").
- *Disposition*: Either add the design-level enumeration file before committing, or soften lines 9/27/563/607 to reference the plan-level register (plan-fmea-enumeration.md) and drop the claim that the standing obligation is discharged.

**[high]** Key question 8 describes the post-trigger window as per-bucket with fields `{afterMs, afterCount}`; the Key-question-6 artifact declares it global, explicitly refuses per-bucket windows, and names the fields `{ms, maxItems}` (report.md:521 vs :185-192).

- *Evidence*: report.md:521: "Yes, as a configurable **per-bucket** post-trigger window — `{afterMs, afterCount}`, whichever fills first". Artifact, report.md:185-191: "/** Trigger discipline — global; per-bucket windows refused. */ trigger?: { ... postTrigger?: { ms?: number; maxItems?: number }; }" with "Post-trigger window before the bundle closes: first of ms / maxItems, **across all buckets**". Q8:521 also says "the window delays delivery by at most `afterMs`" — a field name that appears nowhere in the interface.
- *Disposition*: Rewrite Q8's first sentence to "a single global post-trigger window — `{ms, maxItems}`" and replace the two `afterMs`/`afterCount` mentions with the artifact's names.

**[high]** The global 10–50 MB envelope that I3, Q2 and Risk 6 all describe as configured and statically validated has no field in `RecorderConfig` — no type in the artifact carries the envelope value the validator is supposed to compare Σ maxBytes against (report.md:122-125, :63, :605 vs :158-206).

- *Evidence*: I3 (report.md:124-125): "The global 10–50 MB envelope is enforced STATICALLY by the config validator (Σ maxBytes + header allowance ≤ envelope)". Q2:63: "startup config validation rejects a config whose Σ maxBytes exceeds the envelope". Risk 6 (:605): "**The 10–50 MB envelope is config, not law.**" But `RecorderConfig` (report.md:158-206) declares only `enabled`, `identity`, `buckets`, `redaction`, `trigger`, `nets`, `transport`. The worked example's comment "Σ over all rows validated ≤ envelope (I3)" (:446) likewise has no envelope operand in the config it annotates.
- *Disposition*: Add one field to `RecorderConfig` (e.g. `envelopeBytes: number`, with the header/overhead allowance stated in its doc-comment), or say explicitly in I3 that the envelope is a build-time constant of the validator and correct Risk 6's "envelope is config".

**[high]** Q13 and the artifact's `redaction` doc-comment both promise seal-time redaction of "the config snapshot", but Q9 and `BundleHeader` deliberately replaced the config snapshot with a `configDigest` — the bundle carries no config snapshot to redact, and the two lists of seal-time redaction targets disagree with each other (report.md:555, :177-181, :172 vs :381-382, :525).

- *Evidence*: Q13 (:555): "the seal applies the same rules to the envelope's own free text — the trigger's error message and stack ..., **the config snapshot (can embed secrets)**, and the quarantine ride-along". Artifact `redaction` doc (:178-180): "at seal, the three fields that bypass them: trigger error/stack, **caller context**, **config snapshot**". Q9 (:525): "a **config digest plus the validation residue** rather than the raw config snapshot — ... dissolving the secrets-in-config-snapshot hazard the enumeration flagged"; BundleHeader (:381-382): "Digest, not the raw config"; `readonly configDigest: string`. Note also the two seal-time triples differ: Q13 lists quarantine but omits caller context; the artifact lists caller context but omits quarantine (`CaptureOptions`:313-316 confirms context is redacted).
- *Disposition*: Drop "config snapshot" from both redaction lists (a digest is not redactable) and reconcile the remaining targets to one list: trigger error/stack, caller context, and the quarantine ride-along. Also fix :172's "the bundle's config snapshot must stay truthful" to refer to the digest.

**[medium]** The MQTT quarantine ride-along is promised as a bundle section and as a seal-time redaction target, but `SealedBundle` has no such member and no interface seam lets the mqtt bucket read `quarantine.entries()` at seal (report.md:84, :525, :555 vs :361-364, I5/I6/I7).

- *Evidence*: Q4 (:84): "at seal, the bucket copies `quarantine.entries()` into the bundle as a distinct section". Q9 (:525): "The quarantine ride-along is its own section with the same record discipline." But `export interface SealedBundle { readonly header: BundleHeader; readonly buckets: Readonly<Record<string, SealedBucket>>; }` (:361-364) — no quarantine field. I5 defines seal as "O(n) copy of immutable record references + a per-bucket cut marker" (:129-131) with no external read, I6 is "Observation only" (:132), and I7 says built-ins get no capability the public surface lacks (:137-140) — yet reading the boundary's quarantine ring at seal is exactly such a capability.
- *Disposition*: Either add an optional quarantine section to `SealedBundle` plus the mechanism that fills it (most consistent: the composition-root transport adapter reads it at assembly time, as Q9 already routes 0070's IngressStats), or delete the ride-along claims from Q4/Q9/Q13.

**[medium]** Q10's delivery counters (`deliveryFailed`, plus the counted drop when a third seal evicts the older pending bundle) exist in no type, and the transport seam as specified gives the recorder no channel to learn about them, so they cannot "ride the next bundle" (report.md:533 vs :200-205, :327-337, :352-357).

- *Evidence*: Q10 (:533): "a third seal ... evicts the older pending bundle **with a counted drop**" and "the drop increments `deliveryFailed`, and the counter rides the next bundle and the last-gasp loss marker". `DropCounters` (:352-357) has exactly evicted/truncated/oversized/projectionFailures; `RecorderHealth` (:327-337) has enabled/configErrors/buckets/triggers/unknownBuckets. The transport doc-comment (:200-205) puts retry and delivery wholly behind the seam — "Its errors are its own to absorb: the recorder never reports through itself" — with a `(bundle: SealedBundle) => void | Promise<void>` signature that returns nothing the recorder reads.
- *Disposition*: Add a delivery counter block to `RecorderHealth` (e.g. `delivery: { failed, evictedPending }`) and a one-line note on how the transport reports it back, or restate Q10 so the delivery counters live in the transport adapter's own block on the wire body (the route Q9 already uses for IngressStats).

**[medium]** Q11's per-tap `tapError` counter, threshold-triggered self-protective detach, and health field `detached: 'self-protective'` contradict the artifact: no such counter or field exists, `detach`'s doc-comment names only two callers (neither is the recorder itself), and the health field is `detachReason` (report.md:544 vs :275-285, :339-349, :352-357).

- *Evidence*: Q11 (:544): "Every tap therefore wraps its entire body in its own try/catch whose failure path increments a per-tap `tapError` counter and, past a threshold, detaches the tap (recorded in health as `detached: 'self-protective'`)". Artifact `detach` doc (:275-278): "**Two callers, one member**: the composition root wires source teardown here ... and a tap that can OBSERVE its own seam die calls it itself". `BucketHealth` (:339-349) has `detachedAt`/`detachReason`, no `detached`; `DropCounters` has `projectionFailures` but no `tapError`.
- *Disposition*: Add `tapErrors` to DropCounters (or say tap-body throws are counted as projectionFailures), name the recorder as a third `detach` caller in the doc-comment, and change Q11's field reference to `detachReason: 'self-protective'`.

**[medium]** The rubric's Boundedness cell for build says "three-species drop counters" where Q2, Q9 and the `DropCounters` type all say four (report.md:584 vs :65, :525, :352-357).

- *Evidence*: Rubric (:584): "strong by design — byte + count caps, **three-species** drop counters, structured markers". Q2 (:65): "**Drop accounting counts four species separately**: evictions ..., truncations ..., oversized ..., and projection failures". Q9 (:525): "the full health snapshot at seal (**four-species** drop ledger ...)". `DropCounters` (:352-357) declares evicted, truncated, oversized, projectionFailures.
- *Disposition*: Change "three-species" to "four-species" in the rubric cell.

**[medium]** Q8 says RD-7's bundle identity "already carries the `supplements` field", but `BundleHeader` carries `baseBundleId` instead — the inverse relation, and no field of that name exists (report.md:521 vs :369).

- *Evidence*: Q8 (:521): "RD-7's bundle identity already carries the `supplements` field the enumeration asked for, so the door is open." BundleHeader (:369): "readonly baseBundleId?: string;  // supplements name their base". Q12's RD-7 restatement (:551) also ends the list with "supplements".
- *Disposition*: Reword Q8 (and RD-7's restatement at :551) to "`baseBundleId`, by which a supplement names its base".

**[medium]** Q12's "All ten reservations survive into the recommended record and bundle shapes" is overstated for RD-7: `BundleHeader` carries no `creator` and no `platform` field, and the config snapshot RD-7 reserves was deliberately replaced by a digest (report.md:551 vs :366-386; plan RD-7 at research-plan.md:415-420).

- *Evidence*: Q12 (:551): "All ten reservations survive into the recommended record and bundle shapes ... RD-7 envelope identity (format version, **creator**, app build, **platform**, **config snapshot**, device id, page-incarnation id, bundle id, supplements)". research-plan.md:415-417 confirms RD-7 reserves "format version, creator, app build, platform, config snapshot, and instance identity". `BundleHeader` (:366-386) declares formatVersion, bundleId, baseBundleId, deviceId, pageIncarnationId, appBuild, clockOrigin, sealedAt, trigger, configDigest, configErrors, health — no creator, no platform.
- *Disposition*: Either add `creator` and `platform` to BundleHeader, or state in Q12 that RD-7 survives with two amendments (creator/platform folded into `formatVersion`+`appBuild`, config snapshot → digest per Q9).

**[medium]** Q3 and Q12 promise `packetId` on every MQTT record, but the chosen tap (0060 wire-1) cannot see it and the report's own worked example omits it — which also undercuts Q12's claim that captures convert into 0070 `RaceHarness.push` input (report.md:76, :551 vs :471; 0060 design.md:283-291; 0070 design.md:135-139).

- *Evidence*: Q3 (:76): "the MQTT scrubber serializes the already-validated payload plus `{topic, packetId, direction}`"; Q12 (:551): "the MQTT record carries `{topic, packetId, payload, direction}` ... the `ValidatedMessage | 'gap'` shape 0070's `RaceHarness.push` consumes". The worked example (:471) records `{ channel: ev.channel, topic: ev.topic, params: ev.params, payload: ev.payload }` — no packetId — and 0060's wire-1 `MessageEvent` (design.md:283-291) is exactly `{type, channel, topic, params, payload}`. `packetId` is constructed inside 0070's ingress ("dedup identity: messageId + topic", 0070 design.md:137), which Q4 explicitly refuses to tap. Q3 also describes an MQTT scrubber while the example declares "no project()" for the mqtt row (:449-450).
- *Disposition*: Drop `packetId` from Q3/Q12's MQTT record shape and note that the RaceHarness conversion needs a packetId synthesized at replay from topic + a message id the wire-1 event does not carry — or move the MQTT tap's stated view, which Q4 rules out.

**[medium]** The elision marker shape in Q3 (`{elided: true, kind, count}`) does not match the `LossMarker` union in the artifact (`{elided, at, cause}`) — the promised count of elided items exists in no type (report.md:75 vs :418).

- *Evidence*: Q3 (:75): "the practical mechanism is a replacer that counts depth/breadth exhaustion during the walk and stamps `{elided: true, kind, count}` on the record's marker block". Artifact (:418): "| { readonly elided: true; readonly at: string; readonly cause: 'depth' | 'breadth' | 'coercion' }". No `count`; `kind` is spelled `cause`; `at` is absent from Q3.
- *Disposition*: Add `count?: number` to the elided variant and rename Q3's `kind` to `cause` (and mention `at`), or drop the count from Q3.

**[medium]** The `record()` pipeline order in the artifact places redaction after bounded serialization, contradicting Q13 and Q3, which put redaction inside the projection walk before metering — and key/path rules cannot be applied to an already-serialized string (report.md:268-271 vs :555, :76, :65).

- *Evidence*: `RecorderTap.record` doc (:268-270): "Inside, in order, before return: project → bounded-serialize (walk guarded ...) → **redact** → meter UTF-8 → truncate-with-copy → stamp". Q13 (:555): "they apply **per bucket at projection time, inside the capture-turn walk**, so redacted values never enter the ring at all". Q3 item 3 (:76): "Per-bucket scrubbers run inside the projection, **before bytes are metered**". `RedactionRules` is `{ keys?: string[]; paths?: string[]; patterns?: RegExp[] }` (:229) — keys and paths presuppose a live object graph, not a JSON string.
- *Disposition*: Reorder the doc-comment to "project → redact → bounded-serialize → meter → truncate-with-copy → stamp", or state that redaction runs as the serializer's replacer (which is what Q13's "inside the capture-turn walk" describes).

**[medium]** Q5 routes the proposed 0060 abort counters "into the bundle's health block", but that block is `RecorderHealth` — the recorder's own state — and Q4/Q9 route every external counter through the transport adapter outside the recorder's surface (report.md:96 vs :385, :327-337, :84, :525).

- *Evidence*: Q5 (:96): "the stats counter as three monotonic integers on the existing `stats` surface (`aborted`, `timedOut`, `settled`), **read at seal into the bundle's health block**". BundleHeader (:384-385): "Full health snapshot at seal ... readonly health: RecorderHealth", and `RecorderHealth` (:327-337) contains only recorder-owned fields. Q4 (:84) and Q9 (:525) put 0070's IngressStats "outside the recorder's surface: the transport adapter — app code assembling the OpenAPI wire body from the `SealedBundle` — reads them at assembly time", precisely to keep the recorder observation-only (I6).
- *Disposition*: Change Q5's phrasing to "read at bundle-assembly time by the transport adapter into the wire body's environment block", matching Q4/Q9.

**[low]** I4's ledger equation is stated in terms (`recorded`, `retained`) that no health or bundle field names, so the invariant the report calls the bundle's accountability guarantee is not checkable from a bundle as written (report.md:127-128, :65 vs :339-357).

- *Evidence*: I4 (:127-128): "The ledger reconciles. recorded = retained + evicted + oversized, and projectionFailures counts entries that never became records." `BucketHealth` (:339-349) exposes `seq` (last stamped sequence number), `count` (instantaneous), `bytes`, `drops` — no cumulative `recorded` and no `retained`. Q2 (:65) repeats the equation as "the ledger invariant is stated in the interface (question 6, I4)".
- *Disposition*: One clause in I4 binding the terms to fields: retained ≡ `BucketHealth.count`, recorded ≡ `seq` (records stamped since construction) — or add a `recorded` counter.

**[low]** Three statements of the envelope arithmetic disagree on their terms: I3 validates "Σ maxBytes + header allowance", while Q2 says the per-entry overhead constant × maxCount is what the envelope arithmetic includes, and neither mentions the other's term (report.md:124-125 vs :66, :210-211).

- *Evidence*: I3 (:125): "(Σ maxBytes + header allowance ≤ envelope)". Q2 (:66): "the ring's per-entry fixed overhead ... is documented as a stated constant multiplied by maxCount, **so the envelope arithmetic can include it**". BucketPolicy (:210-211): "Per-record index/meta overhead is outside the meter and bounded by maxCount (stated, per plan Q2)" — with no mention of a header allowance anywhere in Q2 or Q9.
- *Disposition*: State one formula in both places, e.g. Σ(maxBytes + overheadConst × maxCount) + header allowance ≤ envelope.

**[low]** The `oversized` drop species is unreachable under the artifact's own stated default for `maxEntryBytes`, so a counter the ledger equation depends on can only ever fire on a configuration the validator arguably should reject (report.md:213-215, :65).

- *Evidence*: BucketPolicy (:214-215): "Default min(maxBytes / 8, 256 KiB). An entry whose truncation floor still exceeds maxBytes is dropped and counted oversized." Q2 (:65) defines oversized as "unstorable even at the truncation floor — the boundary case where `maxEntryBytes`' floor still exceeds the bucket budget". With the default, maxEntryBytes ≤ maxBytes/8 < maxBytes always, so no truncated entry can exceed the bucket budget; the species requires an explicitly configured maxEntryBytes > maxBytes, which fail-safe-loud validation would presumably flag as an invalid row.
- *Disposition*: One sentence saying the validator rejects maxEntryBytes > maxBytes (making oversized a defense-in-depth counter), or re-define oversized against the per-record floor (markers + metadata) rather than maxEntryBytes.

### Claims vs primary sources (15)

**[medium]** Claim (1), the pivot of the build-vs-wrap verdict, is overstated as written: report L39 says Sentry's `addBreadcrumb` "pushes the caller's object (message truncated to 2048 chars at add time; nothing else touched)". At 10.70.0 it pushes a *new* shallow-spread object, not the caller's object; the graph pin comes from the nested fields (notably `breadcrumb.data`) that the shallow spread carries over.

- *Evidence*: scope.ts @ 10.70.0, verbatim: `const mergedBreadcrumb: Breadcrumb = { timestamp: dateTimestampInSeconds(), ...breadcrumb, message: breadcrumb.message ? truncate(breadcrumb.message, 2048) : breadcrumb.message }; this._breadcrumbs.push(mergedBreadcrumb);` — so a fresh top-level object plus an injected default timestamp, with nested values by reference. The substantive verdict survives: prepareEvent.ts confirms `const { normalizeDepth = 3, normalizeMaxBreadth = 1_000 } = options;` and normalization touches only `b.data` at event-prep (`breadcrumbs: event.breadcrumbs.map(b => ({ ...b, ...(b.data && { data: normalize(b.data, depth, maxBreadth) }) }))`), and scopeData.ts merges breadcrumbs with `[...(event.breadcrumbs || []), ...breadcrumbs]` — array copied, objects by reference.
- *Disposition*: Reword L39 (and the matching Recommendation sentence "Sentry's ring stores caller references") to: "`addBreadcrumb` pushes a shallow-spread copy whose nested values — notably `data` — remain the caller's live references; only `message` is touched (truncate 2048), and normalization of `data` is deferred to event-prep." One clause; verdict unchanged.

**[medium]** Claim (5)'s companion footgun is misstated: report L43 says an out-of-range Bugsnag `maxBreadcrumbs` "**silently** falls back to the default 25", and the Boundedness rubric row (L584) scores "silent fallback-to-default on misconfig". The fallback is real but not silent — a warning is logged.

- *Evidence*: packages/core/config.js: `maxBreadcrumbs: { defaultValue: () => 25, message: 'should be a number ≤100', validate: value => intRange(0, 100)(value) }`. packages/core/client.js `_configure`: `if (!valid) { accum.errors[key] = schema[key].message; accum.config[key] = defaultValue }` followed by `if (keys(errors).length) { this._logger.warn(generateConfigErrorMessage(errors, opts)) }` — default substituted AND a console warning emitted.
- *Disposition*: Drop "silently" in L43 and "silent fallback-to-default" in the rubric row; say "falls back to the default 25 rather than clamping (logged as a config warning, not enforced)". The dominated verdict rests on the 1 MB ceiling and is unaffected.

**[low]** Bugsnag source claims are pinned to `master`, but master is not 8.10.0 — the version the report says it evaluated (L41, L43, and the rubric).

- *Evidence*: https://raw.githubusercontent.com/bugsnag/bugsnag-js/master/packages/core/package.json reports `"version": "8.9.0"`. All Bugsnag file citations in Sources (json-payload.js, config.js, client.js, delivery-xml-http-request) are `.../master/...`, while every other candidate is tag-pinned (sentry-javascript/10.70.0, safe-stable-stringify/v2.5.0, xstate@5.32.5).
- *Disposition*: Re-cite the Bugsnag files at the `v8.10.0` tag, or add one clause noting master was read at 8.9.0 and the cited code is unchanged in 8.10.0.

**[low]** Claim (3) narrows the attachment item payload type: report L33 says attachment items "carry `Uint8Array`". The type is a union.

- *Evidence*: types/envelope.ts @ 10.70.0: `export type AttachmentItem = BaseEnvelopeItem<AttachmentItemHeaders, string | Uint8Array>;`
- *Disposition*: Optional: "carry `string | Uint8Array`". Does not affect the object-mapping conclusion.

**[low]** Claim (6) describes the keepalive quota as shared "across all in-flight keepalive-class requests"; the spec scopes it to the request's client's fetch group, not the whole browser/origin.

- *Evidence*: Fetch, HTTP-network-or-cache fetch: inflightKeepaliveBytes is summed over "the set of fetch records in group whose request's keepalive is true and done flag is unset", where group is httpRequest's client's fetch group; "If the sum of contentLength and inflightKeepaliveBytes is greater than 64 kibibytes, then return a network error." (whatwg/fetch issue #1816 quotes the step verbatim; the spec's note explains the bound.)
- *Disposition*: Optional precision at L527: "shared across the page's in-flight keepalive requests (per fetch group)". The design consequence — one 64 KiB budget for the last-gasp path — is correct as stated.

**[low]** Claim (2) verified: breadcrumb overflow records a `buffer_overflow` client-report outcome, flushed as a client_report envelope item through the same transport on visibilitychange-hidden.

- *Evidence*: scope.ts: `if (this._breadcrumbs.length > maxCrumbs) { this._breadcrumbs = this._breadcrumbs.slice(-maxCrumbs); this._client?.recordDroppedEvent('buffer_overflow', 'log_item'); }`. client.ts: `recordDroppedEvent(reason, category, count = 1) { if (this._options.sendClientReports) { const key = `${reason}:${category}`; ... } }`. browser/src/client.ts: `WINDOW.document.addEventListener('visibilitychange', () => { if (WINDOW.document.visibilityState === 'hidden') { if (sendClientReports) { this._flushOutcomes(); } ... } })`. Two conditions the report does not spell out and need not: the scope must have a bound client, and the recorded data category is `log_item`.
- *Disposition*: no fix needed — verified correct

**[low]** Claim (3) verified: a custom Transport receives the structured Envelope tuple, not serialized bytes.

- *Evidence*: types/transport.ts @ 10.70.0: `export interface Transport { send(request: Envelope): PromiseLike<TransportMakeRequestResponse>; flush(timeout?: number): PromiseLike<boolean>; }`. types/envelope.ts: `type BaseEnvelope<EnvelopeHeader, Item> = [EnvelopeHeader & BaseEnvelopeHeaders, Array<Item & BaseEnvelopeItem<BaseEnvelopeItemHeaders, unknown>>];`, `type BaseEnvelopeItem<ItemHeader, P> = [ItemHeader & BaseEnvelopeItemHeaders, P];`, `export type EventItem = BaseEnvelopeItem<EventItemHeaders, Event>;` — so the plan-era "envelope protocol as the wire format" correction stands.
- *Disposition*: no fix needed — verified correct

**[low]** Claim (4) verified: `Sentry.init` without a DSN constructs no transport and installs no integrations, so a wrap needs a dummy DSN (L37).

- *Evidence*: client.ts @ 10.70.0: `if (options.dsn) { this._dsn = makeDsn(options.dsn); } else { DEBUG_BUILD && debug.warn('No DSN provided, client will not send events.'); }`; `if (this._dsn) { ... this._transport = options.transport({...}); }`; `public init(): void { if (this._isEnabled() || this._options.integrations.some(({ name }) => name.startsWith('Spotlight'))) { this._setupIntegrations(); } }`; `protected _isEnabled(): boolean { return this.getOptions().enabled !== false && this._transport !== undefined; }`. One unstated exception — a Spotlight integration still sets up without a DSN — is irrelevant to the wrap.
- *Disposition*: no fix needed — verified correct

**[low]** Claim (5) verified: Bugsnag's json-payload.js strips event metadata client-side above 1,000,000 chars and replaces it with a warning string inside the payload; the published API 400s anything still over 1 MB, and the spec's current payload version is 5 against the SDK's hardcoded 4.

- *Evidence*: packages/core/lib/json-payload.js verbatim: `if (payload.length > 10e5) { event.events[0]._metadata = { notifier: `WARNING!\nSerialized payload was ${payload.length / 10e5}MB (limit = 1MB)\nmetadata was removed` }; payload = jsonStringify(event, ...) }`. SwaggerHub bugsnag-error-reporting-api (openapi 3.0.3): Bugsnag-Payload-Version "Must be set to `5`"; 400 response described as "The payload was empty or too large (greater than 1MB)."
- *Disposition*: no fix needed — verified correct

**[low]** Claim (7) verified: Chromium refuses a sendBeacon Blob whose type is not CORS-safelisted, per the BCD note, which is still current.

- *Evidence*: api.Navigator.sendBeacon.json note, verbatim: "Starting in Chrome 59, this method cannot send a <code>Blob</code> whose type is not CORS safelisted. This is a temporary change until a mitigation can be found for the security issues that this creates." — carried on Chrome 59+, Chrome Android 59+, Opera 46+ and the other Chromium entries. Firefox sendBeacon version_added 31, matching the report's "sendBeacon FF31+" rubric cell.
- *Disposition*: no fix needed — verified correct

**[low]** Claim (8) verified: CompressionStream is floor-safe at Chromium 80+ / Firefox 113+ (L527, rubric L578).

- *Evidence*: api.CompressionStream.json: constructor version_added chrome 80, firefox 113, safari 16.4; the gzip format entry carries the same versions.
- *Disposition*: no fix needed — verified correct

**[low]** Claim (9) verified, including the line numbers: fast-redact 3.5.0 compiles accessors with the `Function` constructor, so it throws under a CSP without `unsafe-eval`.

- *Evidence*: lib/redactor.js line 9: `  const redact = Function('o', \`` (line 8 is the eslint-disable comment). lib/restorer.js line 19: `    this.restore = Function(` ... `    ).bind(state)`. Both match the report's "[lib/redactor.js line 9, lib/restorer.js line 19]" exactly.
- *Disposition*: no fix needed — verified correct

**[low]** Claim (10) verified in full at v2.5.0: the elision markers are in-band strings, BigInt defaults to String(value), and the array-breadth off-by-one drops one element with no marker.

- *Evidence*: index.js @ v2.5.0: depth exhaustion emits `'"[Object]"'` / `'"[Array]"'`; object breadth appends `'"...":${whitespace}"${getItemCount(removedKeys)} not stringified"'` and array breadth appends `` `${join}"... ${getItemCount(removedKeys)} not stringified"` ``; circularValue default `'[Circular]'`; `case 'bigint': if (bigint) { return String(value) }` with `bigint` defaulted to false only inside the strict branch (readme: "bigint … Default: true"; "strict … Circular values and bigint values throw as well"). Array path: `const maximumValuesToStringify = Math.min(value.length, maximumBreadth)` … `if (value.length - 1 > maximumBreadth) { const removedKeys = value.length - maximumBreadth - 1; res += … }` — at length === maximumBreadth + 1 the guard is `M > M` (false), so element index M is dropped unmarked.
- *Disposition*: no fix needed — verified correct (the spike's re-test in question 15 remains the right hedge, since this was read from source, not executed)

**[low]** Bonus check (report L513): the correction of the plan's "Chromium 139+" crash-reporting claim is sound.

- *Evidence*: Chrome release notes 139, verbatim: "This feature ensures developers receive only crash reports by specifying the endpoint named `crash-reporting`. By default, crash reports are delivered to the `default` endpoint which receives many other kinds of reports besides crash reports." Chrome's Reporting API doc: "If you want to receive intervention, deprecation, crash reports or a combination of these, set an endpoint named `default`." — 139 adds only the dedicated endpoint name, consistent with Reporting-Endpoints (BCD chrome 96) carrying crash reports to `default` all along.

**[low]** Bonus check (report L37, L39, L592): `globalHandlersIntegration` really does write `Error.stackTraceLimit = 50` globally, and the default integrations really do wrap fetch/XHR/console/DOM.

### Constraint and charter compliance (15)

**[high]** The report claims the plan's one standing obligation (question 15: re-run the D-0036-shape enumeration over the recommended design) is discharged and cites `design-fmea-enumeration.md` three times — but that file does not exist in the track directory.

- *Evidence*: Report line 563: "**The standing obligation is discharged beside this report**: ... its register is [design-fmea-enumeration.md](design-fmea-enumeration.md)"; line 9: "the design-level failure-mode enumeration beside this report names the hazards that spike must exercise"; line 607: "The plan's question 15 obligation is discharged: the design-level failure-mode enumeration ran over this recommendation and its register sits beside this report." Plan line 490-493 makes this a standing obligation. `ls tracks/0160-flight-recorder/` returns only: design-panel-record.md, plan-fmea-enumeration.md, report.md, research-plan.md. `git status` shows only design-panel-record.md and report.md as new untracked files — the enumeration register is neither tracked nor untracked.
- *Disposition*: Either land the register file beside the report before committing, or strike the three discharge claims and re-mark question 15's obligation as outstanding. The report must not ship asserting a deliverable that is absent.

**[high]** D-0004 breach: the verdict not to pay for the outbound-MQTT capture gap rests on an app fact — outbound publish volume — that is neither in facts/app-profile.md nor in the report's declared-assumptions list.

- *Evidence*: Report line 95: "the gap is acceptable for the initial recorder because **the app's outbound MQTT volume is a small fraction of traffic** and every outbound publish is app-initiated". facts/app-profile.md's MQTT section records "Rough peak message rate (msgs/sec): ~50 per second" with no inbound/outbound split and no publish-rate fact anywhere; the intake register (2026-08-22-0160 items a–l) raises no outbound-volume item; and the report's "Assumptions in place of facts" list (lines 611-620) does not carry it. CLAUDE.md: "where a fact is missing, a report declares the assumption it made in its place."
- *Disposition*: Add a declared assumption ("outbound publish volume is a small fraction of the ~50 msg/s aggregate — assumed, not supplied") and either raise it as a new intake item or restate the question-5 verdict so it does not lean on the unverified figure.

**[medium]** Question 8 contradicts question 6's interface and the design panel's recorded ruling: it specifies a per-bucket post-trigger window `{afterMs, afterCount}`, while the recommended interface has a single global `postTrigger: { ms, maxItems }` and per-bucket windows are explicitly refused.

- *Evidence*: Report line 521: "Yes, as a configurable **per-bucket** post-trigger window — `{afterMs, afterCount}`, whichever fills first". Report line 185-191 (the interface): "/** Trigger discipline — global; per-bucket windows refused. */ ... postTrigger?: { ms?: number; maxItems?: number };" and line 190 "across all buckets". design-panel-record.md line 31 records the rejection: "V2's per-bucket postTrigger rows — the post-trigger window catches the aftermath of ONE incident, which is not a per-stream property; one global {ms, maxItems} default zero (V1's refusal, unrebutted)."
- *Disposition*: Rewrite question 8's first sentence to "a configurable global post-trigger window `{ms, maxItems}`" and keep the plan's per-bucket phrasing only as the rejected alternative, citing the panel's reason.

**[medium]** Question 11 rules a behaviour — a per-tap `tapError` counter and threshold-triggered self-protective detach — that appears nowhere in the recommended interface, which is the track's deliverable; the interface's own detach doctrine names only two callers, neither of them a threshold rule.

- *Evidence*: Report line 544: "Every tap therefore wraps its entire body in its own try/catch whose failure path increments a per-tap `tapError` counter and, past a threshold, detaches the tap (recorded in health as `detached: 'self-protective'`)". But `DropCounters` (lines 352-357) carries only evicted/truncated/oversized/projectionFailures; `BucketHealth` (339-349) has no tapError field; and `detach` (lines 275-285) documents "Two callers, one member: the composition root ... and a tap that can OBSERVE its own seam die". `grep tapError design-panel-record.md` returns nothing — the panel never saw this element. It is also the only recorder-evaluated threshold rule in the design, adjacent to the charter's excluded class (D-0040 ruling 1: "recorder-evaluated threshold rules ... were considered and excluded") even though it gates a detach rather than a trigger.
- *Disposition*: Add `tapError` to `DropCounters`/`BucketHealth` and the `detach` doc comment (a `'self-protective'` reason), and state in question 11 that the threshold governs tap self-protection only — never a trigger — so the charter's exclusion is visibly untouched.

**[medium]** The rubric's Boundedness cell describes the build's drop accounting as "three-species", contradicting the four-species ledger the report specifies everywhere else — including the criterion's own evidence base.

- *Evidence*: Rubric line 584: "strong by design — byte + count caps, **three-species** drop counters, structured markers". Question 2, line 65: "**Drop accounting counts four species separately**: evictions ..., truncations ..., oversized ..., and projection failures"; question 9, line 525: "the **four-species** drop ledger"; `DropCounters` (lines 352-357) declares four fields.
- *Disposition*: Change "three-species" to "four-species" in the Boundedness row.

**[medium]** Question 12 prescribes periodic `getPersistedSnapshot` checkpoint records "on a config cadence" for the xstate bucket, but no cadence knob exists anywhere in the recommended config, and the mechanism is recorder-invoked app code on a timer — the shape the panel refused when it rejected recorder-invoked probes.

- *Evidence*: Report line 551: "for the xstate bucket that means the periodic `getPersistedSnapshot` checkpoint row rrweb's checkout pattern prescribes, **on a config cadence**, so a transitions-only ring can always be replayed from its own contents". `RecorderConfig` and `BucketPolicy` (lines 158-227) carry no cadence field; `RecordMeta.checkpoint` (line 309) is only a per-record flag stamped by the tap. Report line 102 records the panel's refusal of "a tap lifecycle registry with recorder-invoked probes ... probes are more recorder-executed app code to contain", and I6 (line 132) states "The recorder subscribes to nothing, publishes nothing, classifies nothing".
- *Disposition*: Either add the cadence to the xstate tap's own (app-side) configuration and say plainly that the tap — not the recorder core — owns the timer and the `getPersistedSnapshot` call, or downgrade RD-9's xstate answer to "the tap stamps checkpoint on snapshot-bearing records it already observes" and name periodic snapshotting as unresolved.

**[medium]** A plan question-4 sub-question is silently skipped: the connect-time replay burst that a `clean: false` session delivers immediately on connect, and whether the wire-1 tap is attached before it arrives.

- *Evidence*: Plan lines 260-266: "everything emitted before a bucket attaches is never captured — **including the connect-time replay burst a `clean: false` session delivers immediately**, and `xstate.init`". The report's ordering answer (line 89) says only "wire-1 listener after boundary construction" and prices "the boot window before attachment" to question 7's pre-arm stub — which covers pre-boot JS errors, not broker traffic. `grep -n "replay burst\|clean: false" report.md` returns no hits. This matters because the app runs a persistent session (facts/app-profile.md: "`clean: false` with a persisted clientId") whose reconnect backlog is exactly the history a bundle would want.
- *Disposition*: Add one sentence to question 4's "When taps attach" bullet stating whether boundary construction connects eagerly, and therefore whether the wire-1 tap must be attached before `boundary` construction completes (or that the first-connect backlog is a declared capture gap).

**[low]** Line 68 sources "few-KB payloads" to facts/app-profile.md; the profile carries no payload-size fact at all — that is intake item a, and the report elsewhere correctly declares it as an assumption.

- *Evidence*: Report line 68: "the report's worked defaults (question 6) are declared assumptions sized against the ~50 msg/s aggregate and few-KB payloads **in the app profile**". facts/app-profile.md's MQTT section lists rate, QoS, session mode, protocol, broker — no payload sizes; intake 2026-08-22-0160 "## a — Typical and worst-case payload sizes" is open. Report line 611 does declare it: "MQTT payloads and REST bodies are few-KB typical / low-tens-of-KB worst case (item a)".
- *Disposition*: Reword to "the ~50 msg/s aggregate in the app profile and the few-KB payload assumption standing in for intake item a".

**[low]** Intake item d (initial buckets and depths; whether xstate machines carry a `version`) is neither answered nor listed among the declared assumptions, although the worked bucket table and RD-7's "machine id+version" reservation both depend on it.

- *Evidence*: Intake register: "## d — Initial buckets and depths" ... "also which xstate machines matter and whether machines carry `version`". The report's assumptions list (lines 611-620) resolves items a, b, c, e, f, g, i, j, k, l — d is absent. Yet the worked config (lines 442-456) fixes maxCount/maxBytes per bucket, and question 12 line 551 reserves "machine id+version" as if it exists.
- *Disposition*: Add one assumption line: "The worked bucket table and depths are placeholders pending item d; machines are assumed to carry an id and may not carry a version — RD-7 records whichever exists."

**[low]** Equal-merit tilt in the rubric: the build scores "strong" on Browser compatibility because its primitives were floor-checked, while all three SDK candidates score "adequate" with the explicit note that their floors were "not re-verified here" — the differential rests on the survey's own non-verification rather than on evidence about the candidates.

- *Evidence*: Rubric line 578: build "strong — every primitive floor-checked this survey"; Sentry "adequate — ... ES/browserslist floor not re-verified here"; Bugsnag "adequate — legacy-broad by reputation; FF124 floor not re-verified here"; Faro "adequate — modern-evergreen posture; FF124 floor not re-verified here". The same shape appears on Runtime overhead (line 581), where build's "strong — ~6.5 KB gzip of deps **plus the owned kit**" leaves the owned kit unquantified while Sentry is scored against a measured ≤33 KB.
- *Disposition*: Keep the scores but say in the paragraph under the table that the Browser-compatibility and Runtime-overhead differentials are partly evidence-asymmetric (build measured, candidates unverified/unquantified), so no verdict weight is placed on those two rows — the verdict already rests on capture-safety and Boundedness.

**[low]** D-0040 ruling 2 says the vendored OpenAPI contract exists so that "D-0006's validation discipline applies to it"; the report narrows that to build/test-time validation and skips runtime validation. The narrowing is argued, not silent, but the report does not acknowledge that it is narrowing the charter's language.

- *Evidence*: D-0040: "**The report endpoint exists; the payload schema is ours**, to be authored as a vendored OpenAPI contract so 0010's pipeline and D-0006's validation discipline apply to it." Report line 537: "validated at the schema level by 0010's generated validators **at build/test time** ... runtime validation of an outbound diagnostic POST adds cost in exactly the degraded moment and is explicitly skipped, with the reasoning recorded." D-0006's own text is inbound-scoped ("validating every inbound message from any contracted interface"), so the report's reading is defensible on the decision's letter.
- *Disposition*: Add half a sentence: "D-0006's letter is inbound-scoped, so this is outside it rather than an exception to it; D-0040's phrasing is read as build/test-time discipline for an outbound contract."

**[low]** No fix needed — verified correct: the charter's passive-observer ruling (D-0040 ruling 1) holds throughout the design. Nothing classifies and nothing auto-triggers.

- *Evidence*: Triggers are exactly the charter's two classes: `capture(reason)` (line 252, "Entry point 2 — the ONE trigger path. Explicit app calls and every net land here identically") and the config-armed nets `windowErrors`/`unhandledRejections` (line 198) plus an app-mounted `<RecorderErrorBoundary>` (lines 428-430) — the report is explicit that React 18.3.1 "has no root error hook, so this is a component the app mounts, not a hook the recorder arms". I6 (line 132): "The recorder subscribes to nothing, publishes nothing, classifies nothing, participates in no transaction". The one recorder-armed platform listener — the `visibilitychange`-hidden loss marker (line 527) — is scoped to "a loss marker, not a mini-bundle ... only when a sealed bundle is in flight or pending", which the plan pre-authorized as delivery, not a new trigger (plan line 350-351).

Delivery: line 533 keeps the null and rules its open half ("bounded retries, then drop with a counter"), and line 535 refuses parking against the charter's three-part bar — "**Durable parking fails its burden of proof at survey depth** ... The verdict: **no durable parking in the recommended design**" — with a named revisit trigger. Replay: question 12 (line 551) carries RD-1..RD-10 and the two pre-commitments only, and the rubric contains no replay-rewarding criterion.

D-0003: the two adopted deps are MIT (safe-stable-stringify 2.5.0, tracekit 0.4.9 — lines 20-21), and the one licence hazard found is flagged and routed around — "the loader template lives in the FSL-1.1 getsentry/sentry repo, source-available but not OSS, so verbatim reuse fails D-0003; the pattern is ~50 lines and reimplementable" (line 517). Rubric: all nine plan criteria appear at the plan's weights (License/Maintenance/TS fit/Browser compat/Integration/Runtime/Escape hatch high, Output quality medium, Contract-format n-a) plus "Boundedness (high, D-0041)", across all four postures (lines 573-584).

## Appendix B — all 203 failure modes (verbatim)

Each mode exactly as its lane returned it. The `Owner` strings are the lanes' own verdicts
against the design, not the adjudication above.

### Capture path — record(), projection, metering, truncation, eviction (24 modes)

#### capture-path-1 — record() pipeline — redaction placement (RecorderTap.record order vs Q13)

- **Mode** *(high)*: The interface applies redaction to the finished serialized string while Q13 says it runs inside the projection walk; the two cannot both hold, and each reading breaks something different.
- **Mechanism**: report.md:266-272 fixes the order "project → bounded-serialize (walk guarded) → redact → meter UTF-8 → truncate-with-copy", so redact receives a JSON string. Q13 (report.md:555) states the rules "apply per bucket at projection time, inside the capture-turn walk, so redacted values never enter the ring at all". On the string reading, `keys`/`paths` rules — the worked config uses `paths: ['payload.operator.*']` (report.md:440) — are structural and cannot be evaluated against a flat string without re-parsing (a second full walk plus a second full copy), and a `patterns` substitution over raw JSON text can match across syntax and corrupt the body. On the structural reading, the applier must either walk the app object a second time (re-running getters whose second read may differ from the one that was serialized) or fuse into the serializer's replacer — which Q3 has already claimed for elision detection.
- **Effect**: Implementer picks silently. Either the full plaintext (secrets included) is materialized as a string on the capture turn before any rule runs, or path-scoped rules quietly no-op — and path rules are the design's ONLY per-bucket scoping mechanism after per-bucket rule sets were refused (report.md:180-181), so every bucket collapses to the same global key/pattern set with no error raised.
- **Owner**: `partial:I2/Q13 — the interface's stated pipeline order contradicts Q13's placement, and neither text says which is normative`
- **Rationale**: Q13 owns "whose rules, applied where, marked how" and answers "per bucket at projection time, inside the capture-turn walk"; the capture interface — the artifact Q6 calls "the interface's real content" — orders redact after bounded-serialize. The contradiction is inside the recommended design, not inherited from the plan, and the plan-level pass only found that redaction must cover non-bucket content (shortlist 11), which Q13 does answer.

#### capture-path-2 — record() pipeline — redaction applier cost

- **Mode** *(high)*: Config-supplied RegExp rules run per record on the capture turn with no bound on pattern complexity, pattern count, or scanned length.
- **Mechanism**: `RedactionRules.patterns?: RegExp[]` (report.md:229) is app config in a codebase the report itself characterises as loose-TS with heavy agent-authored churn. The applier runs inside record(), against bodies up to `maxEntryBytes` (default `min(maxBytes/8, 256 KiB)`, report.md:215) at ~50 records/s. A pattern with nested quantifiers backtracks superlinearly on a 256 KiB body; even linear patterns scan N_patterns × body × 50/s. I1's try/catch sees throws, never a stall.
- **Effect**: Unbounded synchronous stall inside 0060's shared dispatch turn, which sheds for every consumer at 256 — the recorder degrades the app through the one capture-path step whose cost the report never budgets, and the drop ledger records nothing because nothing threw and nothing was evicted.
- **Owner**: `UNOWNED`
- **Rationale**: Q11's per-hazard list (report.md:543-547) budgets tap bodies as "project, meter, push, return" and enumerates self-capture, throw containment, seal spike and the kill switch — redaction is absent. Q13 (555) rules where the rules apply, never what they cost. The spike list (563) measures "projection cost on live app-shaped objects"; the redaction applier over near-maxEntryBytes bodies is not on it.

#### capture-path-3 — bounded serialization — elision marker generation

- **Mode** *(high)*: The prescribed mechanism for turning safe-stable-stringify's in-band elision into structured markers cannot observe the elisions it must mark.
- **Mechanism**: Q3 point 2 (report.md:75) prescribes "a replacer that counts depth/breadth exhaustion during the walk". In safe-stable-stringify 2.5.0 the breadth cap sorts and then SLICES the key list before iterating, so elided keys and array elements are never passed to the replacer at all; the array off-by-one the Survey itself found (report.md:20) emits no in-band string either, so nothing is observable by any means; depth elision is inferrable only if the replacer reconstructs its own depth from the holder object, which the report does not describe. The stated fallback — detecting the library's `"[Object]"` / `"... N items not stringified"` output — cannot distinguish those from app strings with identical content. Because keys are sorted before slicing, breadth elision also keeps the lexicographically-first N keys, so a wide record retains `channel`/`params` and drops `topic`.
- **Effect**: The wrapper the Recommendation rests on (report.md:590, "wrapped so its in-band elision becomes structured markers") is not buildable as specified; the design ships either missing markers — RD-5's well-formed-wrong-evidence anti-pattern one layer down, which Q3 exists to prevent — or an owned walk, which changes the micro-dep's rung-1 justification and the bundle-size story.
- **Owner**: `partial:Q3 — the requirement is owned and correct; the named mechanism does not work against the chosen serializer, and the spike re-tests only the array off-by-one`
- **Rationale**: The plan pass (shortlist 7) forced "serializer elision/coercion carries structured markers" and Q3 adopted it, so the requirement is answered; what the design adds — and gets wrong — is the implementation route. Q15's falsification list names re-testing the off-by-one but not whether the replacer can see breadth exhaustion at all.

#### capture-path-4 — bounded serialization — marker taxonomy

- **Mode** *(medium)*: `LossMarker` has no cause for circular references, so the one elision the chosen serializer emits most stays in-band.
- **Mechanism**: report.md:415-418 defines `{elided: true; at: string; cause: 'depth' | 'breadth' | 'coercion'}`. The Survey (report.md:20) records that safe-stable-stringify renders cycles as the in-band string `"[Circular]"`. A cycle is neither a depth nor a breadth exhaustion nor a JSON coercion, so no marker can legally be stamped for it.
- **Effect**: Cyclic structures — the xstate actor graph and reused Ajv `ErrorObject`s, i.e. exactly the shapes that forced serialize-at-capture — degrade to a bare `"[Circular]"` sitting where a value belongs, indistinguishable from an app string with that content, with every drop counter at zero.
- **Owner**: `partial:Q3 — the rule ("never only in-band strings", report.md:221) is stated, but the type that carries it omits the cause the serializer emits for the design's motivating data shapes`
- **Rationale**: Q3 owns projection-loss marking and the design's own type is the enforcement point; this is a defect the design created when it enumerated the cause set.

#### capture-path-5 — eviction/ring accounting — what the bucket is charged

- **Mode** *(high)*: Metering precedes truncation in the stated order, leaving the charged byte figure ambiguous; the natural reading makes one oversized entry evict the entire bucket.
- **Mechanism**: The order is "…redact → meter UTF-8 → truncate-with-copy → stamp → evict-oldest to fit" (report.md:266-272). The meter therefore returns the PRE-truncation size, which is also what RD-5's `originalSize` needs. `CapturedRecord.bytes` is documented as "metered UTF-8 size of body" (report.md:410) — the post-truncation body. Nothing states that the truncated copy is re-metered, or that truncation happens in the byte domain so the cut offset IS the new size. If the ring is charged what the meter returned, a 12 MB projection truncated to 64 KiB charges 12 MB, and the eviction while-loop drains the whole bucket to make room for a 64 KiB record; if it is charged the post-truncation figure but decremented on eviction using the other, the byte total drifts monotonically.
- **Effect**: Either one pathological payload wipes 100 s of history and books the losses as ordinary ring pressure (DropCounters.evicted merges the causes), or I3's `bytes ≤ maxBytes` and I4's ledger silently diverge from reality. Both are invisible to a metered-bytes property test that never truncates.
- **Owner**: `partial:I2/I3 — both invariants are asserted, but the pipeline order the interface publishes does not produce them, and no text names the re-meter`
- **Rationale**: I3 requires bytes ≤ maxBytes at every instant and I2 requires truncation to copy; the ordering that implements them is spelled out in record()'s doc comment and is where the arithmetic goes wrong. The plan pass found the retention hazard (shortlist 10), not the charging hazard.

#### capture-path-6 — record() pipeline — truncation-with-copy

- **Mode** *(medium)*: Truncating a serialized JSON body at a UTF-8 byte offset produces an unparseable body that still declares `encoding: 'json'`.
- **Mechanism**: `body` holds the serialized record and `encoding` may be `'json'` (report.md:409-411); truncation cuts to `maxEntryBytes` measured in UTF-8 bytes. A cut at an arbitrary offset leaves unterminated strings and unclosed objects, and can split a multi-byte UTF-8 sequence (decode yields U+FFFD) or a UTF-16 surrogate pair (a lone surrogate, escaped as `\udXXX` when the bundle is serialized). Nothing in Q2, Q3 or RD-5 specifies structure-aware truncation or re-serialization of a pruned structure.
- **Effect**: The largest, most interesting records arrive as syntactically invalid JSON labelled as JSON. Q12's standing commitment that the HTTP bucket stays "losslessly projectable to HAR 1.2 entries" and the HAR → @mswjs/source regression path (report.md:551) break on exactly those records, and any consumer that `JSON.parse`s bodies throws instead of degrading.
- **Owner**: `partial:RD-5/Q12 — RD-5 marks that truncation happened; nothing preserves the body's own declared encoding contract, and Q12's projectability commitment is not qualified for truncated records`
- **Rationale**: RD-5 was designed against HAR's silent `-1` sentinel — the marker is present, so the loss is not silent; what is unowned is that the retained remnant no longer satisfies the record's own `encoding` field or the format-level pre-commitment Q12 makes.

#### capture-path-7 — record() pipeline — truncation-with-copy (I2 verification)

- **Mode** *(high)*: I2 mandates a copy but names no mechanism, and the property test that would verify it runs on the wrong engine.
- **Mechanism**: I2 (report.md:120-122) says truncation "COPIES, never slices" and cites SlicedString/dependent strings. The idioms that look like copies are engine-dependent no-ops: V8 returns the same string for `'' + s` and short-circuits `s.repeat(1)`; `slice` yields a SlicedString; `replace`/concatenation yield ConsStrings that retain BOTH operands, so a redaction applier that rebuilds a body by concatenation retains every slice it was built from — I2's copy rule is written about truncation only. The one robust route is a TextEncoder/TextDecoder byte round-trip, which the report never states. Q15's retention property (report.md:563, WeakRef-based) runs on "the accepted fast-check lane" — Node/V8 — while the fleet floor is Firefox/SpiderMonkey, whose dependent-string and rope flattening rules differ, and the same section concedes no real-browser lane exists.
- **Effect**: The invariant that closes the plan pass's highest-severity capture finding is asserted, unspecified, and verified on an engine the fleet does not run. A metered 64 KiB record can pin a multi-MB parent for its ring lifetime on Firefox while the V8 property test passes green.
- **Owner**: `partial:I2/spike — the invariant is owned and the retention test is scoped, but the mechanism is unspecified and the named lane exercises the wrong engine`
- **Rationale**: Plan shortlist 10 forced "truncation must force a copy; Q15's properties test retention, not just the meter", and the design adopted both sentences. What it did not do is name a copy primitive or notice that its only verification lane is V8-only — a gap the design's own lane choice creates.

#### capture-path-8 — metering — what the meter does not count

- **Mode** *(high)*: Marker blocks are unbounded per record and explicitly outside the byte meter, while the design's own default redaction posture generates one marker per leaf.
- **Mechanism**: Q2 (report.md:66) declares per-entry overhead "a stated constant multiplied by maxCount". But `markers?: readonly LossMarker[]` (report.md:412) carries one entry per redaction `{redacted, ruleId, at}` and per elision `{elided, at, cause}`, each with a path string. Q13's declared assumption until intake b answers is "payloads redact to structural skeletons (keys kept, leaf values redacted)" (report.md:555) — i.e. one redaction marker for EVERY leaf. A 200-leaf payload therefore carries 200 markers whose serialized size plausibly exceeds the redacted body's.
- **Effect**: The dominant per-record memory term is neither metered nor constant. The static envelope check (I3, Σ maxBytes + header allowance ≤ envelope) certifies a table whose true resident and wire size is a multiple of maxBytes, and `BucketHealth.bytes` understates the bucket by a traffic-dependent factor — the ring is not bounded by construction in the currency the design claims.
- **Owner**: `partial:Q2 — the overhead rule is stated as a fixed per-entry constant, which the report's own default redaction posture and its own elision-marker design falsify`
- **Rationale**: Q2's fourth bullet is the answer to the plan pass's "the meter states what it does with per-entry index/metadata overhead". The answer is adequate for seq/clocks/flags and wrong for markers, which the design added afterwards in Q3 and Q13 without revisiting the meter.

#### capture-path-9 — metering — ring bytes vs wire bytes

- **Mode** *(high)*: Every byte budget is denominated in ring bytes, but each record's body is re-escaped when embedded in the bundle's own JSON, inflating the POST well past the envelope.
- **Mechanism**: `CapturedRecord.body` is a `string` holding serialized JSON (report.md:411) and the bundle is itself JSON POSTed to the endpoint, so every `"` and `\` inside every body is escaped a second time — typical JSON re-escapes at roughly 1.4–1.7×, control characters at 6×. The 10–50 MB envelope, I3's Σ maxBytes validation, the worked table's 34 MiB, and the assumption that the endpoint "accepts tens of MB" (report.md:613) are all expressed in the ring's metered UTF-8 bytes.
- **Effect**: A table validated as in-envelope produces a materially larger POST — plus unmetered markers and header — so an endpoint ceiling negotiated from the envelope number (intake c) rejects bundles at exactly the sizes worth reporting, and the 5–10× gzip planning ratio is applied to the wrong base. The failure appears only in the field, on the largest bundles.
- **Owner**: `UNOWNED`
- **Rationale**: Q2's honesty caveat covers metered-UTF-8 vs UTF-16 heap retention (report.md:61); Q9 covers compression, the endpoint ceiling and the last-gasp 64 KiB budget (527); neither converts ring bytes to wire bytes, and the double-encoding is a consequence of the design's own choice to type `body` as a string.

#### capture-path-10 — eviction/envelope — resident ceiling

- **Mode** *(high)*: The validated envelope bounds one copy of the records, while the runtime holds up to three copies for minutes.
- **Mechanism**: I3 validates Σ maxBytes + header allowance ≤ envelope, once. At runtime the resident set is: the live rings (which keep filling — nothing pauses capture after a seal), plus the sealed in-flight bundle's records, plus the at-most-one pending bundle Q10 permits (report.md:533), each up to one envelope of the same strings; with bounded retries at ~8 attempts / ~5 minutes the co-residency is sustained, not transient. Q9's streaming-assembly budget (report.md:529) caps only the assembly peak at "envelope + largest bucket". Multiply by the report's own "heap retention can run near 2× the metered figure" caveat and, per Q16, by N tabs.
- **Effect**: A table validated against a 50 MB envelope can hold ~150 MB of records and substantially more heap for minutes, on a RAM-disk device where the report itself says a full RAM disk is an OS-level failure. The config validator — the load-bearing control named in Risk 6 — certifies this as in-budget.
- **Owner**: `partial:I3/Q9/Q10 — each stage is bounded separately (static Σ check, assembly peak, two delivery slots); nothing sums them into a resident ceiling the operator can size the envelope against`
- **Rationale**: The plan pass found the transient seal spike (shortlist 4) and Q9 answers it with streaming assembly. What the design adds is a delivery-slot rule that keeps whole sealed bundles resident for the retry horizon while capture continues, and no text reconciles that with the envelope the validator enforces.

#### capture-path-11 — record() pipeline — transient allocation

- **Mode** *(high)*: `maxEntryBytes` bounds what the ring keeps, not what the capture turn allocates; the whole projection is materialized before any size check.
- **Mechanism**: The order is serialize → redact → meter → truncate. The serializer builds the complete string first; the meter then materializes bytes (TextEncoder.encode allocates a Uint8Array of the full length; Blob semantics likewise); truncation cuts only afterwards. Depth/breadth bounds constrain the walk's SHAPE, not leaf size — a single 12 MB base64 string, or a `toJSON` returning a large string, passes every bound. No early-abort-at-N-bytes serializer is specified anywhere.
- **Effect**: One pathological entry allocates tens of MB on the capture turn inside 0060's dispatch turn, and steady state runs two full-size allocations per record at ~50/s of GC pressure. On the degraded device the recorder is meant to observe, this is an OOM inside the observer — while I3 still reads "bounded by construction".
- **Owner**: `partial:Q3/Q11 — Q3 bounds the walk and Q11 budgets tap bodies as "no allocation beyond the record", which is exactly the assumption that fails; no bound on the pre-truncation transient exists`
- **Rationale**: Q11's capture-cost bullet and Q15's spike measure per-record cost on app-shaped objects — a sample of typical operands. Nothing bounds the worst case, and the design's own maxEntryBytes machinery is applied after the allocation it would have prevented.

#### capture-path-12 — record() pipeline — re-entrancy

- **Mode** *(high)*: The projection walk can re-enter record() on the same recorder, and nothing anywhere declares a re-entrancy guard.
- **Mechanism**: The walk executes app code by design (I1, Q3 point 1). Any getter, Proxy trap or `toJSON` that logs re-enters through 0050's facade → the recorder's log `Sink` (Q4, report.md:87) → `record()` on the log bucket, synchronously inside the outer record(), with the byte total, the seq counter and the eviction while-loop all mid-update. Mutual recursion (the log bucket's own projection logging) terminates only at a RangeError, which can land in any frame — including inside the guard's own bookkeeping, after the outer record has been partially accounted.
- **Effect**: Corrupted byte accounting (I3 breached with no counter able to see it), duplicated or skipped sequence numbers (breaking I5's `seq > cut.seq` post-trigger rule and RD-1 ordering), or a stack overflow that surfaces as an uncaught error into the very dispatch turn I1 promises never to throw into — which then fires the recorder's own net.
- **Owner**: `UNOWNED`
- **Rationale**: I1 covers throws, I2 references, I3 bounds, I4 the ledger; none addresses re-entrancy, and no key question raises it. Q11's three self-capture cuts (report.md:545) are the HTTP marker header, a `sealing` boolean for nets, and never-report-through-itself — none is a record() guard. The design created this path by putting the log facade's sink and the app-code-executing walk on the same synchronous surface.

#### capture-path-13 — record() pipeline — walk-guard escapes (stalls)

- **Mode** *(high)*: A getter or Proxy trap that is slow rather than throwing is invisible to the try/catch and unbounded in time.
- **Mechanism**: I1's guard catches throws. A getter that busy-loops, lazily initializes, decrypts, or computes its value by walking a large structure simply runs to completion on the capture turn; a Proxy `get`/`ownKeys` trap can do arbitrary work per property, multiplied by `maximumBreadth`. Cost is not a function of the record's serialized size, so every figure in the report (0.44 µs / 17 µs, and anything the spike measures on shaped-but-passive operands) is a sample, not a bound.
- **Effect**: Unbounded synchronous stall in 0060's shared dispatch turn or 0070's one-JS-turn pipeline; app-wide shedding attributable to the observer, with nothing counted — no throw, no drop, no marker.
- **Owner**: `partial:Q3/Q11 — the plan named "throw, stall, or mutate"; Q3's design text (report.md:74) keeps only the throw ("a revoked immer draft throws mid-walk") and Q11's budget presumes bounded per-record work`
- **Rationale**: Q3 point 1 is the design's answer to the plan pass's "the serialize walk executes app code" finding, and it answers exactly one third of it. The spike (Q15) measures projection against live app-shaped objects, which samples the cost distribution but cannot bound the tail.

#### capture-path-14 — record() pipeline — walk-guard escapes (side effects)

- **Mode** *(medium)*: Reading every property of live app objects changes app state and app scheduling; the recorder is not the passive observer I6 claims.
- **Mechanism**: The projection reads every enumerable property of live objects taken from wire-1 payloads, xstate inspection events and app-declared entries. Lazy getters that memoize assign on read; proxy-based reactive stores register a dependency on whatever reaction or render is active when a property is read; dev-mode proxies count or warn on access. record() is called from inside the app's own dispatch and render-adjacent turns, so those registrations attach to real app subscribers.
- **Effect**: Extra re-renders or reactions, lazily-initialized state resurrected at a time the app did not choose, dev warnings attributed to app code — app behaviour changes because the recorder is watching, and no one attributes the regression to the observer. I6's "observation only" is broken by the projection itself.
- **Owner**: `UNOWNED`
- **Rationale**: I6 constrains the recorder's OUTPUTS (subscribes to nothing, publishes nothing, never routes through a tapped fetcher); it says nothing about the side effects of reading.
