The recorder is constructed first and has no dependencies (Q6), so passing a three-line fan-out — devtools plus a recorder tap — at kit construction creates no init cycle and reshapes no interface. The report's refusal reasons that "it is a single function slot … and taking it evicts devtools", which is true of the recorder taking the slot and false of the composition root owning it; the design panel record explicitly leaves multiplexing "to the adapter/composition layer" (design-panel-record.md:52,460).
- **Effect**: With only cumulative counters, a message can appear in the mqtt bucket, be dropped by the ingress as duplicate/stale/masked, and leave no trace but a counter delta read at the wrong time (see the IngressStats mode). A reader sees the message arrive and the machine not react, and concludes the state machine is broken. The verdict per message — dedup, guard, mask, dispatch, gap — is the join between the two buckets the recorder exists to correlate.
- **Owner**: `partial:Q4 — the trade is stated and its reversal route named, but the stated reason for it does not survive the design's own construction order`
- **Rationale**: Q4 says "verdict-per-message capture is explicitly traded away for composability, and if that trade ever reverses, the multiplex must be designed as a 0070 extension under its one-selector-per-stream bar". The multiplex needs no 0070 extension at all: it is a function literal in the composition root the report already prescribes.

#### taps-and-hosts-25 — I6 / Q10 — the app-supplied transport and the log bucket

- **Mode** *(medium)*: "The recorder never reports through itself" binds the recorder, not the app-authored transport, and the log bucket is the return path.
- **Mechanism**: `transport: (bundle: SealedBundle) => void | Promise<void>` is supplied by the composition root; Q10 puts bounded retry (default 8 attempts over a ~5-minute horizon) behind it. App code logs, and 0050 is the app's logging facade — so a transport that logs its retry failures emits records that the recorder's own log sink captures into the log bucket, which then rides the next bundle.
- **Effect**: During an endpoint outage the log ring (2 MB in the worked example) fills with recorder-delivery chatter at exactly the moment it should hold application evidence, and each subsequent bundle carries a growing account of the recorder's own failures. Bounded, but it displaces the evidence the bucket exists for.
- **Owner**: `partial:I6 — states the rule for the recorder's internals while the transport that would break it is outside the seam by design`
- **Rationale**: Q11's third self-capture cut says "the recorder never reports through itself — its own failures are counters in the bundle, never `record()` calls", which is enforceable inside the kit. The transport is explicitly the posture boundary and is app code; nothing in the interface, the integration checklist, or D-0002's layering discipline tells its author not to log.

### Identity, schema, and the backend contract (25 modes)

#### identity-and-schema-1 — Interface artifact — CaptureOptions → BundleHeader (Q6, Q9, Q13)

- **Mode** *(high)*: The caller-supplied trigger `context` has no destination anywhere in the sealed bundle.
- **Mechanism**: `CaptureOptions` declares `context?: Record<string, unknown>` and its own comment says "error, stack, and context all pass the redaction rules at seal"; `RecorderConfig.redaction` names "caller context" as one of the three seal-time targets; Q13 repeats it. But `BundleHeader` declares only `formatVersion, bundleId, baseBundleId, deviceId, pageIncarnationId, appBuild, clockOrigin, sealedAt, trigger{reason, coalescedReasons, error{message,stack,markers}}, configDigest, configErrors, health`, and `SealedBundle` is `{header, buckets}`. No member holds `context`.
- **Effect**: The one field a call site uses to explain why it triggered — machine id, pump id, retry count — is either silently discarded at seal or ships in an undeclared field the vendored OpenAPI contract will not carry. Every bundle loses the app's own account of the trigger, and a redaction clause is spent guarding a field that does not exist.
- **Owner**: `partial:Q13 — the seal-time redaction ruling and the config comment both name caller context; Q6's artifact and Q9's header enumeration have no slot for it`
- **Rationale**: Q9's header inventory lists trigger block, app build, config digest + residue, health, clocks, identity — context appears in neither the prose inventory nor the type. The artifact and the ruling are out of sync, and the type is the thing the OpenAPI contract is seeded from ("the OpenAPI contract's seed").

#### identity-and-schema-2 — Redaction scope (Q13) × identity/meta strings (RecordMeta, BundleHeader, RecorderHealth)

- **Mode** *(high)*: Every app-authored free-text string in the bundle bypasses redaction, because the whole-bundle ruling enumerates only three targets.
- **Mechanism**: `RedactionRules`' comment fixes the seal-time set: "the three fields that bypass them: trigger error/stack, caller context, config snapshot". Outside that set and outside every bucket `project()`/serializer (which see `entry`, never `meta`) sit: `capture(reason: string)` and `trigger.coalescedReasons`, `detach(reason)` → `detachReason` in every later bundle, `RecordMeta.correlationId/kind/outcome/mimeType`, the keys of `health().unknownBuckets`, and `configErrors` strings. Under loose TS with agent-authored churn the expected idioms are `capture(`sync failed for ${user.email}`)` and `correlationId: url` (the natural join key for a request/outcome pair is the URL).
- **Effect**: PII and credentials egress on exactly the path Q13's "redaction covers the whole bundle" amendment claims to have closed, and intake item b's constraints — which the report says gate shipping — are unenforceable over it.
- **Owner**: `partial:Q13 — the ruling closed header free text and quarantine; it never reached meta, identity, or health strings`
- **Rationale**: Q13's amendment lists "the trigger's error message and stack…, the config snapshot…, and quarantine ride-along". `openAtSeal` (a `readonly string[]` of caller-supplied correlation ids), `unknownBuckets` keys, and `reason` are all caller-controlled text that reaches `BundleHeader`/`SealedBucket` without passing any scrubber.

#### identity-and-schema-3 — Redaction rule set (Q13) × LossMarker (RD-10)

- **Mode** *(medium)*: `RedactionRules` carries no rule identity, so RD-10's required `ruleId` cannot be produced from the declared config.
- **Mechanism**: `export interface RedactionRules { keys?: string[]; paths?: string[]; patterns?: RegExp[] }` — three anonymous arrays. `LossMarker` requires `{ readonly redacted: true; readonly ruleId: string; readonly at?: string }`. The applier must synthesize the id: an array index (which re-maps the moment a rule is inserted at position 0 — the expected edit under agent churn, silently re-labelling every historical bundle) or the rule's content (the key name or the RegExp source, which echoes the matched field name or the secret's shape into the bundle the rule exists to sanitize).
- **Effect**: RD-10's claim — that a replayer can knowingly substitute synthetic values and a reader can tell which rule fired — degrades to unstable or self-describing ids; cross-version aggregation of redaction markers at the backend is meaningless.
- **Owner**: `partial:Q12 — RD-10 is listed as "survived into the recommended shapes" with the marker typed, but the rule shape it must reference has no id column`
- **Rationale**: Q12: "RD-10 redaction markers `{redacted, ruleId}` distinct from truncation". The marker is realized; the identity it points at is not defined anywhere in `RecorderConfig`.

#### identity-and-schema-4 — Redaction applier (Q13) — pattern rules

- **Mode** *(high)*: A `g`- or `y`-flagged RegExp in `patterns` makes redaction skip every other match, silently.
- **Mechanism**: `patterns?: RegExp[]` is validated and frozen at construction and the same RegExp objects are reused for every record at ~50/s. `RegExp.prototype.test`/`exec` and `String.replace` with the global or sticky flag mutate and retain `lastIndex` on the shared object, so the next call starts mid-string. `/token/gi` is unremarkable authoring; nothing in the config validator's described scope (bucket rows, Σ maxBytes) inspects regex flags, and no invariant requires the applier to clone or reset the pattern.
- **Effect**: Roughly half the records ship the secret, and the other half carry a `{redacted, ruleId}` marker — so the bundle looks correctly scrubbed while leaking, which is worse than no redaction because it defeats review.
- **Owner**: `UNOWNED — Q13 rules where redaction applies and how it is marked; nothing normalizes, validates, or clones pattern state, and I8's validation is described only over bucket rows and the envelope sum`
- **Rationale**: Q13: rules are "fast-redact-style field/path/pattern data… with an owned applier". The owned applier is the whole risk surface here and the report specifies nothing about its handling of stateful RegExp objects.

#### identity-and-schema-5 — Capture pipeline order (RecorderTap.record) vs Q13's application point

- **Mode** *(high)*: The interface places redaction after serialization, where two of the three rule kinds have no defined meaning.
- **Mechanism**: `record()`'s stated order is "project → bounded-serialize (walk guarded) → redact → meter UTF-8 → truncate-with-copy". After bounded-serialize the record is a JSON string. `keys` and `paths` (`'payload.operator.*'`) are object-graph rules; over text they either no-op or force a re-parse of every record inside 0060's dispatch turn. The only kind that applies naturally to a string is `patterns`, and substring replacement inside a JSON document can straddle a quote or `","` boundary and emit text that is no longer valid JSON. Q13 says the opposite — "apply per bucket at projection time, inside the capture-turn walk" (i.e. a replacer, structural) — so the two texts specify different appliers.
- **Effect**: Either key/path redaction silently no-ops on every bucket (secrets ship with zero markers), or capture-turn cost doubles in the seam with the hardest latency budget, or a record body is corrupted into something the backend's generated validator rejects — which, per the delivery mode below, is indistinguishable from an outage.
- **Owner**: `partial:Q13 — states the application point as inside the walk; Q6's artifact states it after serialization, and neither says what a key/path rule means over a serialized record`
- **Rationale**: Two normative statements in the same report disagree about where the applier sits, and the disagreement decides whether two of three rule kinds function at all.

#### identity-and-schema-6 — Redaction applier (Q13) × capture turn (Q11) and seal

- **Mode** *(high)*: Pattern redaction runs unbounded-time inside 0060's shared dispatch turn and again over unbounded header text at seal.
- **Mechanism**: Patterns execute over every projected record at ~50 msg/s inside the shared dispatch turn, and at seal over the trigger's `error.message`/`stack` and `context`, which have no size cap (see the header-boundedness mode). An agent-authored pattern with nested quantifiers backtracks catastrophically on adversarial captured content; JavaScript has no regex timeout, and Q11's per-tap `try/catch` with a `tapError` threshold catches throws, not stalls, so the self-protective detach never fires.
- **Effect**: The recorder freezes the dispatch turn, 0060 sheds at 256 for every consumer, and the observer becomes the incident — the single outcome the track's stated failure bias forbids ("silently under-captures is acceptable; degrades the app is not").
- **Owner**: `partial:Q11/spike — Q11 budgets capture cost and the spike measures "projection cost on live app-shaped objects"; neither covers redaction cost over adversarial content, and the rule set itself is intake item b, unanswered at design time`
- **Rationale**: Q11's hazard list and Q15's spike list both name projection; "redact" is a separate stage in `record()`'s own pipeline and appears in no cost budget or spike lane.

#### identity-and-schema-7 — Trigger path (capture → async assembly) vs I2

- **Mode** *(high)*: `CaptureOptions.error` and `context` are retained as live caller references across the async assembly boundary; serialize-at-capture does not cover the trigger path.
- **Mechanism**: I2 is scoped to the record path: "When record() returns, the recorder holds no reference to the caller's entry." I9 makes `capture()` synchronous only *through the seal*, and Q7 puts assembly explicitly after it: "Everything after — assembly, redaction of the trigger header itself (question 13), serialization, optional compression, delivery — is async off the trigger turn." So the caller's error and context objects are held live until assembly runs, and under Q10's delivery policy potentially until the ~5-minute retry horizon expires.
- **Effect**: (a) The header serializes post-mutation state, and the object most likely mutated during error unwinding is the one passed as context; (b) an error closing over a large graph, or a context holding an actor ref, pins arbitrary heap outside the metered envelope — the exact graph-pinning capture the report disqualified Sentry for; (c) a revoked proxy or throwing getter throws during assembly, off the trigger turn, where I1's containment and the per-bucket `projectionFailures` counter do not apply.
- **Owner**: `partial:I2/I9 — both invariants are written over `record()`; no invariant fixes a projection point for the trigger header's caller-supplied objects`
- **Rationale**: The report's own Sentry rejection rests on "pre-trigger history in Sentry's ring is… the mutation-unsafe, graph-pinning capture the charter sweep ruled out" — the recommended design re-admits precisely that on the trigger path, for the objects most tightly coupled to the failure being reported.

#### identity-and-schema-8 — Bundle header boundedness (I3, Q9)

- **Mode** *(high)*: Nothing bounds the header: `error.message`, `stack`, and `context` are unmetered, uncapped, caller-supplied content.
- **Mechanism**: I3 bounds "Per bucket, count ≤ maxCount and bytes ≤ maxBytes" and the envelope statically as "Σ maxBytes + header allowance ≤ envelope" — a validator constant, not a runtime meter. `BucketPolicy.maxEntryBytes` applies to bucket records only. `LossMarker` on `trigger.error` implies header truncation exists, but no policy, limit, or metering point for header material is stated anywhere. Ajv validation errors and libraries that stringify payloads into their messages routinely produce multi-MB `message` strings; `context` is an arbitrary object.
- **Effect**: One `capture()` call can produce a bundle above the envelope and above the endpoint's ceiling (intake c), which is then rejected and dropped after the retry horizon — the trigger destroys its own evidence. "Bounded by construction" is false for the one part of the bundle app code fills directly, and Q9's peak-memory-at-seal budget is computed over bucket bytes that no longer describe the payload.
- **Owner**: `partial:I3 — the invariant's scope is per-bucket plus a static header allowance; there is no `maxHeaderBytes`, no context cap, and no runtime meter on header material`
- **Rationale**: I3's own text draws the line: bytes are "metered once at capture" on bucket records; the header is assembled at seal from objects that never passed a meter.

#### identity-and-schema-9 — Correlation open-set (RecordMeta.correlationId, SealedBucket.openAtSeal)

- **Mode** *(high)*: The correlation open-set is unbounded and, by design, outlives every bound the recorder has.
- **Mechanism**: `phase:'open'` adds an id, `'settled'` removes it, and ids are deliberately retained after their records are evicted ("in-flight-at-seal is explicit even when either half was evicted"), decoupling the set's lifetime from `maxCount`/`maxBytes`. It leaks on every no-settled-record path: a genuinely hung request (the target use case), an outcome record lost to `projectionFailures`/`oversized`, a tap detached between the two halves (detach keeps ring and set), and — under loose TS, where `phase` is optional and defaults to `'settled'` — a custom bucket that stamps `'open'` on both halves or omits it on the first. Nothing caps the set, evicts from it, counts a discarded id, or bounds `correlationId` string length.
- **Effect**: On a multi-day kiosk session a broken settle path at 1 req/s accumulates ~86k unbounded caller strings per day, outside the metered envelope, in a recorder whose entire premise is memory boundedness; every seal copies the whole set into `openAtSeal`, inflating each bundle and each POST.
- **Owner**: `partial:Q6 — the "deliberately unsolved" list admits only that "the bounded correlation open-set needs a sizing rule before the OpenAPI contract freezes `openAtSeal`"`
- **Rationale**: The admission names a missing sizing rule and stops there. Missing with it: an eviction policy, a `DropCounters` species for a discarded id (all four species are record-shaped), and a marker for an over-capacity set — so the first cap added will break I4's "there is no uncounted loss".

#### identity-and-schema-10 — RD-7 realization in BundleHeader (Q12, Q9)

- **Mode** *(medium)*: RD-7's `creator`, `platform`, and `supplements` are prose-only — Q12 asserts all ten reservations survived into the shapes, and three named fields have no member.
- **Mechanism**: Q12 re-lists RD-7 as "format version, creator, app build, platform, config snapshot, device id, page-incarnation id, bundle id, supplements" and claims "All ten reservations survive into the recommended record and bundle shapes". `BundleHeader` has no `creator` (recorder kit name+version — `appBuild` is the *app's* build), no `platform`, and no `supplements`; Q8 separately rests its supplement door on "RD-7's bundle identity already carries the `supplements` field the enumeration asked for", which the artifact does not define (only the inverse `baseBundleId?`).
- **Effect**: A bundle cannot say which recorder build produced it — the most useful field when the producer is agent-authored and churning, and the only way to interpret a record shape whose `schemaVersion` is a constant. It also cannot say which browser arm produced it, though every delivery, net, and crash-coverage conclusion in the report is arm-conditional; and a delivered base bundle can never be amended to point at a later supplement, the property Q8's claim implies.
- **Owner**: `partial:Q12/Q9 — both prose inventories assert the fields; Q6's artifact omits them and no other member carries the information`
- **Rationale**: Partial recovery exists for one of the three: the backend sees a `User-Agent` header on the POST, so platform is inferable server-side (not for a beacon-only loss marker). `creator` and `supplements` have no substitute anywhere in the design.

#### identity-and-schema-11 — RD-8 schema versioning (BucketPolicy.schema)

- **Mode** *(high)*: `schemaVersion` is decorative: nothing relates it to the projection that actually determines the record shape.
- **Mechanism**: `schema: {schemaId, schemaVersion}` is a literal in the composition-root config row (`schemaVersion: '1'`) while the shape it names is produced by `project()` in a different module (`projectInspectionEvent`) or by a built-in scrubber. No validator, test, or codegen relates the two; I8's config validation checks that rows are well-formed, not that a version matches an emitted shape. Under heavy agent-authored churn, renaming a field inside a scrubber is a schema change with the version string untouched — and the report's own artifact already spells built-in identity two ways (`recorder.mqtt@1` in the interface comment, `{schemaId:'recorder.mqtt', schemaVersion:'1'}` in the worked example).
- **Effect**: The mechanism that exists so stored records remain parseable years later records a constant; the first shape drift is discovered as a parse failure over evidence already in the backend, with no way to tell which bundles predate it.
- **Owner**: `partial:Q12 — RD-8 is realized as "config columns"; nothing owns deriving, asserting, or CI-checking the version against the projection that produces the shape`
- **Rationale**: Q12 lists RD-8's enforcement point as "(config columns)" — i.e. the enforcement is that the column exists, which is orthogonal to whether its value is true.

#### identity-and-schema-12 — Format versioning and the vendored OpenAPI contract (Q9, Q10, D-0010)

- **Mode** *(high)*: Three version namespaces coexist with no bump discipline and no generator relating the TS shapes to the contract.
- **Mechanism**: `BundleHeader.formatVersion`, per-bucket `schemaVersion`, and the vendored OpenAPI document's own version are independent strings. Runtime validation of the outbound POST is explicitly skipped (Q10's D-0006 exemption), so the only enforcement is "every sealed bundle validates against the vendored OpenAPI schema via 0010's generated validators in the test lane" — fixtures, not field data. The wire body is a superset of `SealedBundle` assembled by hand-written app code (Q9: "the transport adapter — app code assembling the OpenAPI wire body from the `SealedBundle` — reads [IngressStats] at assembly time"), so no generator produces the contract from these types, and D-0010's oasdiff drift CI diffs the spec against the spec.
- **Effect**: Producer/consumer skew ships silently. Nobody owns bumping `formatVersion` when `CapturedRecord`/`BundleHeader` change, and the hand-written SealedBundle→wire mapping — under loose TS — is a second unversioned schema with no owner at all.
- **Owner**: `partial:Q9 — names `formatVersion` as a header field and routes IngressStats through an app-side adapter; the bump rule, the mapping's ownership, and the type↔contract check have no owner`
- **Rationale**: The report's constraint list claims "the report payload is a vendored contract through 0010's pipeline", but 0010's pipeline generates types *from* a contract; here the contract must be kept in sync *with* hand-authored TS by hand, which is the drift class D-0010's CI does not cover.

#### identity-and-schema-13 — configDigest (Q9) replacing RD-7's config snapshot

- **Mode** *(medium)*: The digest is resolvable only if something retains the exact config each device was served, and no component, owner, or intake item exists for that.
- **Mechanism**: Q9 substitutes "a config digest plus the validation residue rather than the raw config snapshot — the digest satisfies RD-7's attribution need (the resolvable config lives in deployment)". But the effective config is not a build artifact: `enabled: startupConfig.flightRecorder !== 'off'` and `deviceId: startupConfig.deviceId` come from the app's startup-config channel (the ruled kill-switch seam), so it is per-device and per-time. Nothing requires that channel to record what it served, intake item c asks the backend nothing about it, and the report's own Q11 concedes the channel is "a fact to confirm at integration".
- **Effect**: A bundle showing a thinned or surprising capture carries a 64-hex string that resolves to nothing. The attribution RD-7 exists to provide is lost precisely on the fleet-drift cases — a device left on an old bit, a partially rolled-out config — that the digest was introduced to detect.
- **Owner**: `partial:Q9 — states the substitution and its rationale; states no retention obligation, names no owner for it, and adds nothing to intake`
- **Rationale**: The substitution's whole justification is a claim about the deployment environment ("the resolvable config lives in deployment") that the report elsewhere treats as unverified.

#### identity-and-schema-14 — configDigest input (Q9, RecorderConfig)

- **Mode** *(medium)*: The digest is blind to the config members that decide record shape and redaction, and its normalization is unspecified.
- **Mechanism**: `RecorderConfig` holds functions (`project`, `transport`) and `RegExp` objects (`redaction.patterns`). Any JSON-shaped digest drops functions entirely and renders a RegExp as `{}` (its source and flags are non-enumerable). No text specifies the digest's input set, key ordering, or number formatting.
- **Effect**: Two deployments whose projections and redaction patterns differ completely produce an identical `configDigest` — so the digest is blind to exactly the drift with evidentiary and privacy consequence; combined with a constant `schemaVersion`, the bundle carries no evidence whatsoever of what produced its record shapes. In the other direction, an unstable key order manufactures false drift between identical configs.
- **Owner**: `UNOWNED — no text in the report specifies the digest's input, its normalization, or its treatment of non-JSON members`
- **Rationale**: Q9 introduces `configDigest` as a hazard-dissolving substitution ("dissolving the secrets-in-config-snapshot hazard") without specifying the function; the hazard it dissolves is real, but the attribution it claims to keep is not delivered by any digest over this object.

#### identity-and-schema-15 — RD-9 ring self-sufficiency × RD-5 truncation (RecordMeta.checkpoint, SealedBucket.oldestIsComplete)

- **Mode** *(high)*: `oldestIsComplete` can be `true` over a checkpoint that was truncated.
- **Mechanism**: RD-9 is realized as `checkpoint?: boolean` on `RecordMeta`, evaluated at seal as "no evictions yet OR the oldest retained record carries this bit". A checkpoint row is an xstate `getPersistedSnapshot` — the largest record the bucket produces — and it is subject to `maxEntryBytes` (default `min(maxBytes/8, 256 KiB)`) like every other record, so it can be stored truncated-with-copy carrying `{truncated: true, originalSize}` while still carrying `checkpoint: true`. Nothing makes `oldestIsComplete` consider the oldest record's loss markers.
- **Effect**: The bundle asserts a replayable starting point over a prefix of a serialized snapshot. This is RD-5's own anti-pattern — "a truncated capture without a flag replays as a wrong response rather than a refused one" — promoted from one record to the whole window, and it strikes hardest on the machines with the largest context, i.e. the ones worth replaying.
- **Owner**: `UNOWNED — RD-9's stamp and RD-5's marker are defined independently and never cross-checked; no invariant relates them`
- **Rationale**: Q12 calls the xstate checkpoint "the single biggest foreclosure risk" the reservation exists to prevent, and the design's only guard against a bad checkpoint is a boolean the truncation path does not clear.

#### identity-and-schema-16 — RD-9 checkpoint cadence (Q12) vs BucketPolicy (Q6)

- **Mode** *(medium)*: The checkpoint cadence RD-9 depends on has no column in the frozen bucket row.
- **Mechanism**: Q12: the xstate bucket writes "the periodic `getPersistedSnapshot` checkpoint row rrweb's checkout pattern prescribes, on a config cadence". `BucketPolicy` is `{maxCount, maxBytes, maxEntryBytes?, project?, schema}` — no cadence member — and the table is "validated and FROZEN at construction. No runtime add/remove".
- **Effect**: Either the cadence is hard-coded inside the built-in xstate tap — which makes RD-9 unavailable to the app-declared buckets the interface exists to serve, contradicting I7 ("If a built-in needs more, this interface is wrong") — or it is untunable, so the trade it governs (a large checkpoint evicting the very history it makes replayable, since checkpoints consume the same `maxBytes`) cannot be adjusted per fleet.
- **Owner**: `partial:Q12 — the cadence is prose in Q12's RD-9 paragraph with no corresponding column in Q6's row shape`
- **Rationale**: Every other RD that needed a config surface got one (RD-8's `schema` is "required on EVERY row, uniformly"); RD-9's did not, and the frozen-table ruling closes the runtime alternative.

#### identity-and-schema-17 — Drop ledger (I4, DropCounters) × record stream (RD-1)

- **Mode** *(medium)*: In-window absences are positionally invisible: whether a dropped entry consumes a `seq` is unspecified, and `droppedBeforeWindow` covers only the pre-window prefix.
- **Mechanism**: `droppedBeforeWindow: number  // == counters.evicted at cut` is a cumulative FIFO count, while `oversized` and `projectionFailures` remove entries from the *middle* of the stream. Nothing states whether an oversized entry or a failed projection consumes a sequence number, so a consumer reading `records` cannot distinguish a contiguous window from one with holes, nor tell an oversized drop from a projection failure from a post-seal record (`seq > cut.seq` is the only positional rule the design gives).
- **Effect**: The per-bucket array reads as a contiguous window while carrying holes. I4's "a bundle accounts for every absence" holds only in aggregate: the reader learns that four things were lost, never *where*, which is the question a lead-up capture exists to answer.
- **Owner**: `partial:I4/Q12 — the ledger reconciles totals and RD-1 fixes ordering; nothing positions a loss within the stream`
- **Rationale**: I4 states `recorded = retained + evicted + oversized` with `projectionFailures` counting entries that never became records — an accounting identity, deliberately, but the design elsewhere relies on the record stream reading as history (Q1's "the last 200 transitions… as they interleaved").

#### identity-and-schema-18 — Q12 MQTT pre-commitment × LossMarker / CapturedRecord

- **Mode** *(high)*: The MQTT bucket cannot express a gap, so the `ValidatedMessage | 'gap'` RaceHarness bridge is unbuildable and upstream shedding is invisible.
- **Mechanism**: Q12 pre-commits that "the MQTT record carries `{topic, packetId, payload, direction}` plus explicit gap markers in arrival order — exactly the `ValidatedMessage | 'gap'` shape 0070's `RaceHarness.push` consumes". `LossMarker` has three variants (truncated, redacted, elided) and no gap; `CapturedRecord` has no gap `kind`. The interface's own worked mqtt tap records `{channel, topic, params, payload}` — no `packetId` — and MQTT 3.1.1 QoS 0 has no packet id to record. Separately, the recorder's `seq` counts only what it captured, so loss *upstream* of wire 1 (0060's delivery queue shedding at 256 — the failure the recorder most wants to see, and one the recorder's own latency can cause per Q11) yields a perfectly contiguous record stream.
- **Effect**: The named regression bridge cannot be built from a bundle, and the highest-value MQTT diagnosis — "messages were dropped here" — is unrepresentable. Q9's IngressStats counters are read once at assembly time and can say a count, never a position.
- **Owner**: `partial:Q12 — the pre-commitment is prose in both plan and report; no marker variant, record kind, counter, or field realizes it`
- **Rationale**: Q12 calls this one of "two format-level pre-commitments… worth making now", i.e. cheap-now/unrecoverable-later — and it is the one of the two that the artifact does not carry at all.

#### identity-and-schema-19 — Q12 HTTP pre-commitment × Q3/Q4 scrubber × CapturedRecord

- **Mode** *(high)*: HAR projectability is falsified by the template-key identity choice: the record has no URL, no headers, and no typed status.
- **Mechanism**: Q12: "the HTTP bucket's record fields are chosen to be losslessly projectable to HAR 1.2 entries (the regression path HAR → @mswjs/source `fromTraffic` → MSW handlers)". Q3/Q4 define the scrubber as capturing "template-key identity (`GET /v1/plants/{plantId}`), status, and bounded bodies" — no concrete URL, no path params, no query string, no request or response headers. `CapturedRecord` types none of them: status is not a member (it can only live inside the opaque `body` string), and `outcome?: string` is `'fulfilled'|'aborted'|'error'`.
- **Effect**: HAR requires `request.url` and `fromTraffic` keys handlers on it, so `/v1/plants/1` and `/v1/plants/2` collapse to one key and a replayed handler serves the wrong body; a regenerated handler without response headers has no content-type. The reserved door the track spends its replay budget on is closed by a privacy/cardinality decision made two questions earlier, and nothing in the report reconciles them.
- **Owner**: `partial:Q12 — asserts projectability of the typed record; Q3/Q4 remove the fields HAR needs, with no note of the conflict and no field carrying them`
- **Rationale**: Q12 also warns against "HAR's silent `-1` sentinels" as the anti-pattern; projecting this record to HAR necessarily fills url, headers, cookies and sizes with sentinels or fabrications.

#### identity-and-schema-20 — RD-6 (mimeType/encoding) × the projection core (Q3)

- **Mode** *(medium)*: `encoding: 'base64'` has no producer — binary payloads serialize into index-keyed JSON objects.
- **Mechanism**: The projection is safe-stable-stringify over the entry with `maximumDepth`/`maximumBreadth` bounds. A `Uint8Array`/`ArrayBuffer` payload (MQTT 3.1.1 payloads are bytes; nothing in the profile guarantees JSON on all ~40 topics, and REST bodies can be binary) stringifies to `{"0":31,"1":139,…}`, breadth-elided with an `{elided, cause:'breadth'}` marker. No pipeline stage detects binary or encodes it; `encoding` is caller-supplied meta the tap stamps by hand (`encoding: 'json'` in the worked example).
- **Effect**: RD-6 exists because "binary payloads die without it" — and they die anyway, as well-formed wrong evidence at roughly 8× byte inflation against the bucket budget, with the loss marker blaming breadth rather than a missing binary path.
- **Owner**: `partial:Q12 — the RD-6 fields are realized on `RecordMeta`/`CapturedRecord`; the projection stage that would populate `base64` is specified nowhere`
- **Rationale**: Q3's projection contract enumerates four hazards (guarded walk, bounds, per-bucket scrubbers, sibling mutation) and never mentions binary; `body: string` with no binary channel makes the `base64` variant unreachable as designed.

#### identity-and-schema-21 — Backend contract (Q9 dedup, Q7 absence detection, Q9 loss marker) × intake item c

- **Mode** *(high)*: Three load-bearing backend behaviours are asserted as fact in the report and gated by no intake item.
- **Mechanism**: The report states that "the backend upserts on `(deviceId, pageIncarnationId, bundleId)`", that absence-based crash detection is "built by whoever owns the report backend", and that the last-gasp `{…, sealedButUndelivered: bundleId}` beacon "converts 'bundle never arrived' from silence into a positively-reported loss at the backend" — which requires the backend to join an orphan beacon to a bundle that may never arrive. Intake item c asks the endpoint's owner exactly four things: same-origin or cross-origin, maximum payload size, auth, and shared infrastructure. Dedup, retention, schema acceptance, and beacon-join appear in no intake item.
- **Effect**: At-least-once delivery without server-side dedup turns one incident into N and inflates every fleet metric; the loss marker becomes an orphan row nobody queries; the Firefox crash class stays dark. All three are discovered after the recorder ships, and none is on a list anyone has been asked to answer.
- **Owner**: `partial:Q9/Q10 — the semantics are stated "to whoever owns the endpoint"; stating a requirement in a report is not a gate, and the intake file that exists for exactly this purpose does not carry them`
- **Rationale**: The report's own discipline (CLAUDE.md, and the intake file's framing) is that questions only the user can answer go to `intake/` as dated items; items a, b, c gate parts of the survey. These three are the same class and were not filed.

#### identity-and-schema-22 — Delivery loop (Q10) × skipped runtime validation (D-0006 exemption)

- **Mode** *(high)*: A schema-invalid bundle is indistinguishable from an outage, and the failure is deterministic and fleet-wide.
- **Mechanism**: Runtime validation of the outbound POST is explicitly skipped ("runtime validation of an outbound diagnostic POST adds cost in exactly the degraded moment and is explicitly skipped"), leaving the backend as the first validator. Q10's loop is "bounded retries, then drop with a counter — default 8 attempts / ~5 minutes horizon", with no status-code discrimination stated anywhere: a 400 from the endpoint's schema check consumes the full horizon and increments `deliveryFailed`, identically to a network failure.
- **Effect**: One bad field from an agent-authored projection, one schemaVersion skew, or one pattern-corrupted body makes every bundle from that build fail permanently while the client reports it as a delivery problem. The fleet goes silent, the counter points at the network, and it rides bundles that also fail — so the evidence of the failure cannot be delivered either.
- **Owner**: `UNOWNED — Q10 rules retry termination and at-least-once semantics; nothing distinguishes permanent rejection (4xx) from transient failure, and the skipped runtime validation removes the only client-side detector`
- **Rationale**: Q10's own argument for skipping runtime validation is cost in the degraded moment — defensible — but it shifts detection to a channel the design then treats as a binary success/failure, with no 4xx path and no `configErrors`-style residue for a rejected bundle.

#### identity-and-schema-23 — Quarantine ride-along (Q4, Q9, Q13) × SealedBundle / I3

- **Mode** *(high)*: The quarantine section has no type, no budget row, and no projection scrubber.
- **Mechanism**: Q4 ("at seal, the bucket copies `quarantine.entries()` into the bundle as a distinct section"), Q9 ("The quarantine ride-along is its own section with the same record discipline") and Q13 all reference it, but `SealedBundle` is `{header, buckets}` and quarantine is not a config-declared bucket row — so it has no `BucketPolicy`, no `maxBytes`, no `maxEntryBytes`, no `project()`, and no `schema` (RD-8). Its contents are by definition traffic no schema accepted: up to 100 arbitrary raw payloads of unbounded size, copied in at seal.
- **Effect**: I3's envelope arithmetic (Σ maxBytes + header allowance ≤ envelope) omits the section most likely to hold large malformed payloads, added at the exact moment Q9 budgets peak memory; its redaction rests solely on the seal-time pass over data whose structure is unknown; and it ships without a schema id, so the backend cannot version it.
- **Owner**: `partial:Q13/I3 — Q13 adds quarantine to seal-time redaction, but no type carries it, no policy row bounds it, and I3's arithmetic excludes it`
- **Rationale**: The plan-level pass already flagged "quarantine ride-along bytes belonging to no budget" as an un-acted item the surveyor should read before answering Q2/Q9; the design added the redaction answer and left the budget and the type unanswered.

#### identity-and-schema-24 — RecorderHealth in every BundleHeader (Q6 panel adoption)

- **Mode** *(high)*: `unknownBuckets` and `configErrors` are unbounded, caller-keyed, and ride every bundle header.
- **Mechanism**: `unknownBuckets: Readonly<Record<string, number>>` is keyed "by the offending name — under loose TS the typo names itself (each name also lands in configErrors once)", and `attach(bucket: string)` takes a free string. One agent-authored dynamic name — `recorder.attach(`mqtt-${topic}`)`, an entirely natural mistake given the bucket-per-stream mental model — mints a new map key and a new `configErrors` entry per distinct value, forever. Neither structure has a cap, an eviction rule, or a `DropCounters` species, and `health()` is embedded in every `BundleHeader`.
- **Effect**: An unbounded map inside a memory-bounded recorder, outside the byte meter, growing with traffic; every bundle carries a header that grows without limit and embeds caller-derived strings (topics, ids) that no redaction stage covers. The mechanism the design panel adopted so that typos name themselves is simultaneously an unbounded allocator and a data channel.
- **Owner**: `UNOWNED — the per-name ledger is a ruled panel adoption with no cap, no eviction, no metering, and no place in the drop ledger`
- **Rationale**: Q6 records the adoption's rationale ("an aggregate count hides which name was wrong") and its loose-TS motivation, but bounds it nowhere; I3's boundedness invariant covers bucket rings only.

#### identity-and-schema-25 — RD-2/RD-3 clock domain × BucketHealth / SealedBucket.attach

- **Mode** *(medium)*: Liveness timestamps are bare single-clock numbers with no declared domain, and a wall-clock step is neither detected nor marked.
- **Mechanism**: Every record and cut marker carries `{wallMs, monoMs}`, but `BucketHealth.attachedSince/detachedAt/lastRecordAt` and `SealedBucket.attach.*` are bare `number`s with no stated clock. The three-way liveness diagnosis Q6 spells out — never attached / died at a known time / quiet — is read by comparing these against record clocks. On a RAM-disk device the wall clock is established after boot (RTC or NTP) and can step mid-session; `clockOrigin` fixes one wall↔mono mapping for the whole bundle and nothing detects or marks a discontinuity.
- **Effect**: After a step, `clockOrigin`, record `wallMs`, and the liveness numbers describe different clocks: a bundle can show `lastRecordAt` earlier than `attachedSince`, or a tap that reads "quiet" and was not — and correlation with backend or broker timestamps is silently wrong in the direction that matters (the boot window, which Q4/Q7 identify as a prime error class).
- **Owner**: `partial:Q12 — RD-2/RD-3 are realized on `CapturedRecord` and `cut` only; no invariant assigns a clock domain to the health block or marks a wall-clock discontinuity`
- **Rationale**: Q12 states RD-3 as "one shared clock domain (single-threaded runtime; the origin is stamped once in the envelope)" — stamping the origin once is exactly what makes a mid-session step unrepresentable, and the health block never joined the two-clock discipline.

### Cross-cutting and environment (30 modes)

#### cross-cutting-1 — Q16 multi-tab / Q2 envelope enforcement (I3)

- **Mode** *(high)*: The shipped worked defaults are sized for exactly one tab, so the device-level envelope the user actually gave is breached the moment a second tab exists.
- **Mechanism**: The worked config (report.md:444-455) sums to mqtt 16 MiB + http 8 MiB + xstate 8 MiB + log 2 MiB = 34 MiB. I3 enforces "Σ maxBytes + header allowance ≤ envelope" in the config validator, which is a per-recorder-instance check; each tab constructs its own recorder and runs its own validator, and no validator can see another tab's rings. The app profile records the envelope as a device fact ("the rough memory envelope for 0160's in-RAM capture buffers", facts/app-profile.md:304-306). Two tabs = 68 MiB metered, three = 102 MiB, all passing per-instance validation.
- **Effect**: On a RAM-disk device the recorder consumes 1.4×–2× the user's stated whole-device envelope while every instance reports itself in-bounds; "bounded by construction" is true per tab and false per device, which is the unit the constraint was stated in.
- **Owner**: `partial:Q16 — flags N × envelope to intake item j but ships single-tab-sized defaults and leaves I3's validator scope per-instance`
- **Rationale**: Q16 says "N × envelope is the real cost to weigh, on facts" and "the design must never assume one tab" — but the only concrete numbers in the report (the worked example) do assume one tab, and I3's wording ("the global 10–50 MB envelope") does not distinguish per-tab from per-device. The report names the hazard and then ships defaults that realise it.

#### cross-cutting-2 — Q2 the meter's unit / I3

- **Mode** *(high)*: The byte meter measures serialized UTF-8 while the constraint is device RAM, so the recommended defaults can exceed the top of the envelope on a single tab.
- **Mechanism**: Q2 states the honesty caveat itself: "JS strings are UTF-16 internally, so heap retention can run near 2× the metered figure" and "the envelope numbers in config should be read as serialized-payload budgets, not heap budgets". The stored `body: string` is UTF-16 in the engine. 34 MiB metered ⇒ up to ~68 MiB of heap for ASCII-dominant JSON. The user's 10–50 MB envelope was given as a memory envelope for in-RAM buffers, not a serialized-payload budget.
- **Effect**: A configuration the validator certifies as inside a 50 MB envelope can retain ~68 MB of heap at N=1 — the constraint is silently reinterpreted by up to 2× in the recorder's favour, and every downstream sizing figure inherits the reinterpretation.
- **Owner**: `partial:Q2 — the 2× caveat is stated, then no de-rate is applied to the worked defaults or to the validator's constant`
- **Rationale**: Q2 declares the divergence honestly and stops there; nothing converts the declaration into a rule (e.g. validate Σ maxBytes ≤ envelope/2). The report's own defaults are the counterexample to its own caveat.

#### cross-cutting-3 — Q6 RecorderConfig (I3)

- **Mode** *(high)*: I3 enforces a bound the config surface has no field to express, and the envelope is a range rather than a number.
- **Mechanism**: `RecorderConfig` (report.md:158-206) carries `enabled`, `identity`, `buckets`, `redaction`, `trigger?`, `nets?`, `transport` — there is no envelope field. I3 nonetheless says the envelope is "enforced STATICALLY by the config validator (Σ maxBytes + header allowance ≤ envelope)". The envelope must therefore be a compile-time constant inside the kit, and the charter value is "10–50 MB", a range: at 50 MB the 34 MiB default passes, at 10 MB it fails and I8's table-level rule brings the recorder up disabled-loud on every device in the fleet.
- **Effect**: The single most load-bearing number in the design is unstatable through the interface, untunable without a redeploy, invisible in the bundle (configDigest hashes config, not the constant), and undetermined between two values whose consequences are "ships" and "fleet-wide inert recorder".
- **Owner**: `UNOWNED`
- **Rationale**: No key question assigns the envelope constant an owner, a value, or a place in the config. Q2 discusses enforcement policy, Q6's panel record rules on config shape, and neither notices that the shape omits the operand.

#### cross-cutting-4 — Q9 seal and bundle assembly / Q10 delivery

- **Mode** *(high)*: Sealed bundles retain the strings the ring has since evicted, so the memory cost of a seal is not the transient assembly spike the report budgets but a multi-minute retention of up to a full envelope per held bundle.
- **Mechanism**: I5 seals by copying record references ("pointer copies; the strings are shared", Q9). Sharing decays: the ring keeps capturing at ~50 msg/s × few-KB payloads ≈ 150 KB/s, so the 16 MiB mqtt ring turns over in ~112 s. Q10 keeps one bundle in flight plus one pending, with a retry horizon of "8 attempts / ~5 minutes". Within 300 s the mqtt ring has fully turned over ~2.7 times, so the sealed copies' strings are entirely disjoint from the live ring's. Resident worst case = live rings (34 MiB) + two held bundles (up to 34 MiB each) ≈ 102 MiB metered, ≈ 200 MiB heap at the UTF-16 factor, held for minutes.
- **Effect**: Q9's claim that streaming assembly caps the peak "at roughly envelope + largest-bucket rather than 2–3× envelope" is arithmetically wrong for anything past the first ~2 minutes: 2–3× envelope is the steady state under an outage, not the avoided worst case. On a swapless RAM-disk device this is the recorder becoming the incident.
- **Owner**: `partial:Q9 — budgets a transient assembly spike and never accounts for post-seal retention duration; partial:Q10 — prices "the pinned bundle" at one, while its own rule permits two`
- **Rationale**: Q9's peak-memory paragraph reasons only about co-residency during assembly. Q10's rationale for bounded-then-drop explicitly prices "10–50 MB of RAM" for one pinned bundle, which is the right instinct applied to half the slots and none of the retention arithmetic.

#### cross-cutting-5 — Q9 compression failure path

- **Mode** *(high)*: The gzip-worker degrade path rebuilds on the main thread exactly the envelope-sized string that streaming existed to avoid, and it triggers under the memory pressure that killed the worker.
- **Mechanism**: Q9 caps the peak by "assembly streams per bucket into the worker (bucket-sized chunks, transferable buffers) rather than building one envelope-sized string on the main thread", and separately promises "a dead gzip worker degrades to the uncompressed body on the main-thread path, never to silent loss". The fallback has no streaming story: an uncompressed POST body must exist as one main-thread string/buffer. The likeliest cause of worker death or worker-spawn failure is memory pressure or an OOM-adjacent renderer state — the same condition under which building a 34–50 MB string is most fatal.
- **Effect**: The failure path converts a compression outage into a renderer OOM, destroying the bundle and landing in Q7's no-report death class, and takes the app with it — the one outcome the charter's failure bias forbids.
- **Owner**: `partial:Q9 — the degrade path is asserted "total-by-construction" without reconciling it against the streaming cap in the same paragraph`
- **Rationale**: Both claims are in Q9's peak-memory paragraph and contradict each other. The report never states a size ceiling above which the uncompressed fallback refuses rather than allocates.

#### cross-cutting-6 — Q11 self-interference / environment (RAM disk)

- **Mode** *(high)*: The design has no memory-pressure sensor and no pressure response, on a device class where pressure does not degrade gracefully but kills the renderer outright.
- **Mechanism**: Q2 correctly establishes that no UA memory meter exists at the Firefox ~124 floor (`measureUserAgentSpecificMemory` Chromium-only behind COI; `performance.memory` deprecated). The OS runs from a RAM disk (facts/app-profile.md:293-303), so there is no swap and no disk backing — the page cache *is* the RAM budget. Nothing in the design shrinks rings, refuses seals, or sheds buckets as free memory falls; the rings are static-sized at construction and the only lever, `enabled`, requires a reload.
- **Effect**: The recorder's footprint is fixed at exactly the moment adaptivity would matter. Memory exhaustion presents as an immediate renderer kill — no page event, no bundle, app down — which is simultaneously the worst evidence outcome and the forbidden app-degradation outcome.
- **Owner**: `partial:Q11 — enumerates latency, throw containment, self-capture and the seal spike, but its hazard list has no memory-pressure item and no backpressure design; partial:Q2 — rules out the meter honestly, then designs no response to the condition it cannot measure`
- **Rationale**: Q11's five bullets are all CPU/latency/loop hazards plus "the seal is a hazard and is budgeted as one". The budget is a spike measurement, not a runtime valve. The absence of a meter is an argument for a conservative static budget, which the defaults do not apply (see the N-tab and UTF-16 modes).

#### cross-cutting-7 — Q4 quarantine ride-along / I3

- **Mode** *(high)*: The quarantine ride-along enters the bundle outside every bound the design defines — no bucket, no maxBytes, no maxEntryBytes, not in the meter.
- **Mechanism**: Q4: "at seal, the bucket copies `quarantine.entries()` into the bundle as a distinct section", inheriting "the ring's own bound (100)". That bound is a *count*. Quarantine holds raw, schema-rejected traffic — precisely the payloads with no size discipline, since a malformed or oversized message is a common rejection cause. 100 entries × an arbitrary rejected payload is unbounded in bytes; `SealedBundle` has no field for it and `DropCounters` has no species for it, so it is invisible to I4's ledger too.
- **Effect**: A bundle can exceed the envelope, the endpoint's ceiling (intake c), and any delivery budget by an unbounded factor while every counter reads in-bounds — a bundle that validates and lies about its own size. I3's "bounded by construction" has an uncounted hole in exactly the traffic class most likely to be huge.
- **Owner**: `UNOWNED`
- **Rationale**: Q4 designs the ride-along, Q2/I3 scope the meter to bucket records, Q9 lists the ride-along as "its own section with the same record discipline" without giving it a budget, and Q13 only rules that redaction applies to it. No question bounds it.

#### cross-cutting-8 — Q4 quarantine ride-along / I2

- **Mode** *(high)*: The ride-along is the one capture path that holds live references across an async boundary, and the objects it holds are documented to be mutated in place.
- **Mechanism**: I2 guarantees "when record() returns, the recorder holds no reference to the caller's entry" — but the ride-along never passes through `record()`. It is copied at seal from `quarantine.entries()` and, per Q13, its rules are applied "before assembly", which Q7 places off the trigger turn. Between seal and assembly the recorder holds 0060's quarantine entries; the plan's own grounding says "the quarantined Ajv evidence is a shallow copy whose `ErrorObject`s Ajv reuses" (research-plan.md:97-99), and 0060 specifies no freeze or clone.
- **Effect**: Quarantined evidence in the bundle can show error details belonging to validations that happened *after* the trigger, presented as the state at seal — well-formed wrong evidence with zero drop counters, the exact defect class I2 and RD-5 exist to prevent, re-admitted through the one path that bypasses projection.
- **Owner**: `partial:I2 — the invariant is scoped to `record()` and the ride-along is defined outside it; partial:Q4 — designs the copy without saying copy-of-what`
- **Rationale**: The report contradicts itself here rather than omitting: I2's rationale (Ajv reuses ErrorObjects, nothing is frozen) is the same fact that makes the ride-along unsafe, and Q4 cites the quarantine ring's bound while inheriting none of its reference semantics.

#### cross-cutting-9 — Q2 per-entry overhead / Q13 redaction default / I3

- **Mode** *(high)*: Loss markers are unbounded per record and explicitly outside the byte meter, and the report's own declared redaction default generates one marker per leaf value.
- **Mechanism**: Q2 rules "per-entry index and metadata overhead is declared, not metered", characterised as "sequence number, clocks, byte length, marker flags — tens of bytes" and modelled as a constant × maxCount. But `LossMarker` (report.md:415-418) is an array of objects carrying `ruleId` and a path string `at`, and Q13's declared assumption until intake b answers is "payloads redact to structural skeletons (keys kept, leaf values redacted)" with "every application stamps {redacted, ruleId}" (RD-10). A 200-leaf MQTT payload yields ~200 marker objects — kilobytes — against a stated constant of "tens of bytes", for every record.
- **Effect**: In the configuration the report itself declares as the shipping default, unmetered per-record overhead can exceed the metered body, so actual retention runs several times maxBytes while the meter and the validator both certify compliance. I3's per-instant byte bound is false by construction under the default redaction posture.
- **Owner**: `partial:Q2 — the overhead constant is stated at a magnitude the design's own default contradicts; partial:I3 — bounds only what the meter sees`
- **Rationale**: Q2's constant was sized for fixed fields (seq, two clocks, a length, flags). Q13's structural-skeleton default and RD-10's per-application marking make markers variable-length and payload-proportional. Neither question notices the other's consequence.

#### cross-cutting-10 — Q16 multi-tab / Rubric browser-compatibility row

- **Mode** *(medium)*: Identity minting is an unenumerated floor and secure-context dependency, and the report's floor-check list checks a primitive the design refuses while omitting ones it uses.
- **Mechanism**: Q9 mints `pageIncarnationId` as "a UUID minted once per page load" and the worked example shows `bundleId: 'b_01J…'` (ULID-shaped) — two different id schemes with neither generator named. `crypto.randomUUID()` exists from Firefox 95 but **only in secure contexts**; `crypto.getRandomValues` does not have that restriction. The report never states whether the app's origin is a secure context (the broker connector is `wss://`, which an `http://` page may legally open, so the boundary's TLS proves nothing about the page origin). The rubric's compatibility cell lists "TextEncoder, CompressionStream FF113+, sendBeacon FF31+, BroadcastChannel FF38+" — BroadcastChannel is explicitly refused in Q16, and randomUUID, `performance.timeOrigin`, `WeakRef`, `AbortSignal.timeout` and `requestIdleCallback` (plan B) are absent from the list.
- **Effect**: On a non-secure origin `crypto.randomUUID` is undefined and the recorder throws at construction — violating I8's "createFlightRecorder NEVER throws" before any config validation runs — or falls back to `Math.random`, whose collisions break the backend's `(deviceId, pageIncarnationId, bundleId)` upsert key. The rubric row that claims every primitive was floor-checked is not a true statement about the primitives the design touches.
- **Owner**: `partial:Rubric browser-compatibility — the audit list does not match the design's API surface; UNOWNED for the secure-context question and the id generators`
- **Rationale**: Q9 specifies what identity fields exist and Q16 depends on them, but no question enumerates the APIs the design calls against FF124. The floor was treated as a delivery question (keepalive, Reporting API) rather than a whole-design dependency sweep.

#### cross-cutting-11 — Q11 capture cost / Q2 metering

- **Mode** *(medium)*: Metering allocates a full byte copy of every record inside the disciplined path that Q11 promises allocates nothing beyond the record.
- **Mechanism**: Q2 meters "serialized UTF-8 bytes" with "`TextEncoder`/`Blob` semantics" (plan Q2), which for `TextEncoder.encode(str).length` allocates a Uint8Array the size of the record on every capture. Q11's budget says "every tap body is bounded — project, meter, push, return; no allocation beyond the record". At ~50 msg/s × few-KB records that is ~150–600 KB/s of short-lived allocations *in addition to* the record strings, inside 0060's shared dispatch turn.
- **Effect**: Sustained minor-GC pressure on embedded-class hardware lands in the one place the design identified as its hardest latency constraint — 0060 sheds for every consumer past 256. The measured proxy figure ("+TextEncoder ~0.85 µs") captures CPU time and not the allocation channel, so the spike as scoped would not detect it either.
- **Owner**: `partial:Q11 — states a no-allocation budget the metering step contradicts; partial:spike — the projection-cost lane measures time, not allocation rate or GC`
- **Rationale**: The contradiction is internal: Q2 picks a meter that allocates and Q11 promises no allocation. Resolving it needs either an allocation-free UTF-8 length computation or an amended budget; the report picks neither.

#### cross-cutting-12 — Q13 redaction rules / Q11 capture cost

- **Mode** *(high)*: `RedactionRules.patterns` accepts caller-supplied RegExp objects that run inside the capture turn, with no rule on flags, cost, or statefulness.
- **Mechanism**: `RedactionRules { keys?, paths?, patterns?: RegExp[] }` (report.md:229) and Q13 applies them "per bucket at projection time, inside the capture-turn walk". Under the app's very loose TS with heavy agent-authored churn, two mechanisms are the expected case rather than the exotic one: (1) a pattern with nested quantifiers backtracks catastrophically on an adversarial or merely long payload, executing unbounded work inside 0060's dispatch turn; (2) a pattern carrying the `/g` flag is stateful — `RegExp.prototype.test` advances `lastIndex` and returns false on the following call, so a shared `/g` rule silently skips redaction on alternating records.
- **Effect**: Mechanism (1) is app degradation from inside the recorder — the forbidden outcome — and the per-tap try/catch does not contain it, because it is a hang and not a throw. Mechanism (2) is unredacted PII leaving the device on half the records, with `{redacted, ruleId}` markers present on the other half so the bundle looks correctly redacted.
- **Owner**: `UNOWNED`
- **Rationale**: Q13 rules whose rules, where they apply, and how they are marked. It says nothing about flag normalisation (`/g` stripped or `lastIndex` reset), pattern compilation limits, per-record cost caps, or a timeout. The disqualification of fast-redact was about CSP `unsafe-eval`, not about regex safety, so no adjacent ruling covers this.

#### cross-cutting-13 — Q11 loop protection

- **Mode** *(high)*: The re-entrancy guard covers the synchronous seal only, leaving the recorder's own asynchronous failures free to trigger the recorder.
- **Mechanism**: Q11's second cut: "the nets ignore errors whose stack tops in recorder frames during an active seal (re-entrancy flag, not stack parsing on the hot path: a `sealing` boolean suppresses net-triggered re-seals, counted)". Everything after the seal is async by I9 — worker messaging, compression, retry, POST. `transport: (bundle) => void | Promise<void>` returns a promise; a rejected promise the delivery loop fails to `.catch()` (one missing catch, under agent churn) surfaces as `unhandledrejection`, which the armed net converts into `capture()`. The `sealing` flag is false by then.
- **Effect**: A persistently failing transport becomes a self-sustaining trigger source: one bundle per cooldown window, each pinning up to 34 MiB for a 5-minute retry horizon, forever. I6's "the recorder never reports through itself" is stated as a principle with no mechanism behind the async half.
- **Owner**: `partial:Q11 — three cuts named, the async window not among them; partial:I6 — asserts the property without a guard for promise-rejection paths`
- **Rationale**: The report's own design puts all delivery work off the trigger turn (I9) and then guards only the trigger turn. The gap is structural, not a coding slip.

#### cross-cutting-14 — Q6 health() / I5 seal

- **Mode** *(high)*: The at-seal health block is a "snapshot" by description only; `readonly` is compile-time and buys nothing in this codebase.
- **Mechanism**: `health(): RecorderHealth` is documented as "a cheap immutable snapshot", `BundleHeader.health` is "Full health snapshot at seal", and every field is `readonly`. Under TS 5.9 with very loose strictness, `readonly` erases at runtime; the cheapest implementation an agent writes is returning the live counters object. Counters are cumulative and keep incrementing after the seal, during the async assembly/retry window that runs for minutes.
- **Effect**: The bundle's drop ledger, byte totals and tap liveness read post-seal values while presenting as at-seal state — I4's reconciliation (`recorded = retained + evicted + oversized`) will not close against the sealed record array, and a reader diagnosing loss sees evictions that happened after the evidence window. This is exactly the torn-snapshot defect the plan-level pass caught in the ring protocol (shortlist item 3), recurring one layer up in a place the design names with a word rather than a mechanism.
- **Owner**: `partial:I5 — mandates copy semantics for records and cut markers, and is silent on the counters and health object sealed alongside them`
- **Rationale**: I5's text is "O(n) copy of immutable record references + a per-bucket cut marker". `DropCounters` and `RecorderHealth` are neither records nor cut markers, and the only thing standing between them and the same tear is a TS modifier the app's configuration does not enforce.

#### cross-cutting-15 — Q11 throw containment / the recorder's own code under loose TS

- **Mode** *(medium)*: Self-protective tap detachment is per-tap but its likeliest cause is shared code, so a single defect blinds all four buckets at once with no path to notice.
- **Mechanism**: Q11: "Every tap therefore wraps its entire body in its own try/catch whose failure path increments a per-tap `tapError` counter and, past a threshold, detaches the tap." All four built-in taps call the same `record()` implementation — the same bounded serializer wrapper, the same redaction applier, the same meter, the same ring. An agent-introduced defect there (a null dereference on an optional `meta` field, a marker-array push on undefined) throws for every tap on every record, so all four cross the threshold within seconds of each other.
- **Effect**: Fleet-wide total capture loss. The only signal is `health()`, which reaches a human only inside a delivered bundle — and a bundle still delivers, with four empty buckets and `detached: 'self-protective'`, which requires someone to be reading bundles that contain nothing. There is no startup or runtime alarm equivalent to I8's `configErrors.length === 0` assertion for this failure class.
- **Owner**: `partial:Q11 — designs per-tap isolation and a threshold, without addressing correlated failure of the shared implementation behind the taps or an alerting path for it`
- **Rationale**: The isolation contract is correct for the hazard it was written for (a bucket's own `project()` throwing). It is silent on the case where the isolated thing is a facade over shared machinery, which is the common case here since I7 forbids privileged built-ins.

#### cross-cutting-16 — Q9 environment block / Q10 delivery independence

- **Mode** *(high)*: The delivery path is made to depend on the boundary it exists to diagnose, through the transport adapter's stats read.
- **Mechanism**: Q9 routes 0070's six `IngressStats` counters and wire-2 depths through "the transport adapter — app code assembling the OpenAPI wire body from the `SealedBundle` — reads them at assembly time". Q10 separately claims "the report POST is a bare `fetch` outside 0060's boundary, deliberately — the failure being reported is plausibly the boundary itself". The transport adapter must therefore hold live references to the 0060 boundary and the 0070 kit and call into them during assembly, and nothing specifies that the read is guarded.
- **Effect**: If the boundary has been disposed, has thrown, or is mid-recreation — the exact failures the recorder was built to capture — the stats read throws inside the transport, assembly never completes, and the bundle is lost. The design's stated independence holds for the socket and not for the code path.
- **Owner**: `partial:Q9 — introduces the dependency to keep the recorder "observation-only"; partial:Q10 — claims independence for the POST while Q9 has coupled the assembly`
- **Rationale**: The two claims sit in adjacent questions and are not reconciled. I6's "its errors are its own to absorb" is about transport errors, not about the transport failing to produce a body at all; there is no fallback that ships the bundle with the environment block omitted.

#### cross-cutting-17 — Q9 bundle identity / Q10 retry

- **Mode** *(medium)*: It is unruled whether the wire body is assembled once or per attempt, so the same bundleId can carry different content across retries under a backend that upserts on it.
- **Mechanism**: Q9 fixes the dedup key as `(deviceId, pageIncarnationId, bundleId)` with "the backend upserts". Q10 specifies bounded retries (8 attempts / ~5 min) but never says whether the retry re-sends a retained body or re-runs assembly. If it re-assembles, the IngressStats read at assembly time (Q9's "their own read clock") differs per attempt, and post-trigger records under a configured window may differ too. If it retains, the memory arithmetic above binds. Neither is stated.
- **Effect**: Under upsert semantics, which version of a bundle the backend ends up storing is a race between delivery attempts — an evidence-integrity defect that is invisible client-side and unfalsifiable server-side, since all versions carry the same key.
- **Owner**: `UNOWNED`
- **Rationale**: Q9 owns identity and dedup semantics; Q10 owns retry mechanics. Neither states the body's lifecycle across attempts, and the choice has consequences in both questions' domains.

#### cross-cutting-18 — Q10 delivery loop / RAM-disk memory

- **Mode** *(high)*: Bounded-then-drop is counted in attempts, not wall-clock, and no per-attempt timeout is specified — so a hung POST pins the envelope indefinitely.
- **Mechanism**: Q10: "bounded retries, then drop with a counter: default 8 attempts / ~5 minutes horizon". A `fetch` with no `AbortSignal.timeout` and no `AbortController` deadline does not settle when a connection is established and then stalls (a half-open socket to an endpoint whose host is wedged — a common shape when the report endpoint shares infrastructure with the failing backend, which intake item c has not ruled out). Attempt 1 never completes, so the attempt counter never reaches 8 and the ~5-minute horizon is never evaluated.
- **Effect**: One bundle (up to 34 MiB metered, ~68 MiB heap) plus one pending slot stay resident for the life of the tab on a device with no swap — the precise "pinned memory" outcome Q10's own rationale says it chose bounded-then-drop to avoid.
- **Owner**: `UNOWNED`
- **Rationale**: Q10 rules the termination policy and its default numbers but specifies no request deadline, and no other question mentions one. The spike list's delivery lane tests "bounded-then-drop against a scripted dead endpoint" — a dead endpoint refuses fast; a hung one is a different script and is not named.

#### cross-cutting-19 — Q10 delivery / OpenAPI contract drift

- **Mode** *(high)*: The retry loop does not distinguish permanent rejections from transient failures, so a contract drift presents as an outage and destroys the evidence that would explain it.
- **Mechanism**: Q10 specifies backoff, one-in-flight-plus-one-pending, and bounded-then-drop with a `deliveryFailed` counter. Nothing distinguishes 4xx from 5xx/network. Q10 also explicitly skips runtime validation of the outbound body ("runtime validation of an outbound diagnostic POST adds cost in exactly the degraded moment and is explicitly skipped"), so the client has no local check that its body still matches the vendored contract. A backend schema change, or drift in the app-owned transport adapter that assembles the wire body, produces a deterministic 400 on every bundle.
- **Effect**: Every bundle in the fleet is retried 8 times over ~5 minutes — pinning memory pointlessly — then dropped and counted identically to an endpoint outage. The one artefact that would diagnose the drift (the rejected body) is discarded, and the last-gasp loss marker reports only `sealedButUndelivered`.
- **Owner**: `UNOWNED`
- **Rationale**: Q10 owns retry policy and Q9 owns the wire format; neither maps HTTP response classes to loop behaviour, and the validation-skip ruling in Q10 removes the only client-side detector without substituting one.

#### cross-cutting-20 — Process — the vendored OpenAPI contract through 0010

- **Mode** *(medium)*: The payload contract has no author, no sequencing, and two independent type sources with no drift control.
- **Mechanism**: The recommendation names "a vendored OpenAPI payload contract generated through 0010's pipeline" and calls question 6's TypeScript block "the OpenAPI contract's seed". That yields two definitions of the same shape: the hand-written `SealedBundle`/`CapturedRecord`/`BundleHeader` interfaces the kit programs against, and the 0010-generated types/validators the test lane checks against. Nothing states which is authoritative, who writes the OpenAPI document, or when it is written relative to the kit. 0010 is an accepted *survey* under D-0001 — a recommended pipeline, not a built one.
- **Effect**: Under agent churn the two drift silently: a field added to the TS interface but not the document rides the wire and is dropped by the backend (or accepted as an unvalidated extra property); a field renamed in the document fails only in the test lane. And the recorder cannot ship its schema before someone builds 0010's pipeline — a hard build-order dependency on an unimplemented recommendation.
- **Owner**: `UNOWNED`
- **Rationale**: The report explicitly refuses this dependency shape elsewhere: the log bucket "is declared optional and must not create a build-order dependency" on the unbuilt 0050 facade. The same reasoning is not applied to 0010, and the payload schema is not optional.

#### cross-cutting-21 — Q16 duplicate-bundle disposition

- **Mode** *(high)*: Q16 refuses client-side suppression because near-duplicates carry different evidence, then hands them to a backend dedup keyed on fields that cannot tell them apart.
- **Mechanism**: Q16 rejects BroadcastChannel cooldown sharing as "wrong-by-default (suppressing tab B's bundle because tab A reported loses tab B's distinct history — the near-duplicates carry different evidence)" and in the same paragraph specifies "the backend dedups by `(deviceId, trigger time-bucket, reason)` for reporting purposes, which costs nothing client-side". Those three fields are identical across tabs for a device-level cause — that is what makes the bundles near-duplicates. A backend implementing "dedup" on that key keeps one and discards the rest.
- **Effect**: The distinct per-tab history the client refused to suppress is discarded server-side instead, by a rule this report is instructing the backend owners to implement. Either the refusal's reasoning is wrong or the backend instruction is; as written they cancel.
- **Owner**: `partial:Q16 — contradicts itself within one paragraph; "for reporting purposes" is ambiguous between grouping and discarding and is the only thing holding the two claims apart`
- **Rationale**: The correct instruction is grouping, not deduplication — the distinct dedup key `(deviceId, pageIncarnationId, bundleId)` from Q9 already exists for at-least-once duplicates. Q16 introduces a second, lossier key without saying it is for display only.

#### cross-cutting-22 — Q16 multi-tab cost accounting

- **Mode** *(medium)*: "Costs nothing client-side" is false: N tabs multiply upload volume on a device whose network carries the traffic being diagnosed.
- **Mechanism**: Q16 prices N near-duplicate bundles as a backend concern. Each bundle is up to 34 MiB uncompressed (Q9 ships uncompressed by default until intake c confirms gzip decoding), retried up to 8 times over ~5 minutes. Three tabs reacting to one device-level cause is up to 3 × 34 MiB of concurrent POST traffic, competing on the same link with the MQTT stream (~50 msg/s) and the REST boundary.
- **Effect**: The recorder's reaction to an incident can starve the app's own transport during the incident — degrading the app, not merely duplicating evidence — and can extend or manufacture the very boundary symptoms the bundle is meant to explain.
- **Owner**: `partial:Q16 — states the duplicate cost only in bundles-at-the-backend terms; partial:Q11 — its self-interference hazard list has no network-contention item`
- **Rationale**: Q11 enumerates CPU, throw, loop and seal hazards. Bandwidth is a shared resource on the same device and is nowhere treated as one, even though Q10's independence argument already establishes that the report endpoint may share fate with the app's backends.

#### cross-cutting-23 — Q9 header / RD-7 attribution

- **Mode** *(medium)*: Replacing the config snapshot with a digest makes bundles uninterpretable at the byte level, because no digest→config registry exists.
- **Mechanism**: Q9 ships "a config digest plus the validation residue rather than the raw config snapshot", justified because "the resolvable config lives in deployment". RD-7 reserved a config snapshot precisely so a bundle is self-describing. With only a digest, a reader holding a bundle cannot know any bucket's `maxCount`/`maxBytes`/`maxEntryBytes` — so `evicted`, `truncated` and `droppedBeforeWindow` counts cannot be converted into a time window or a coverage claim, which is the entire diagnostic use of the ledger.
- **Effect**: The health block degrades from evidence to trivia unless someone builds and operates a digest→config registry across a fleet of agent-churned deployments. Nobody is assigned that, and the report treats the deployment's resolvability as given.
- **Owner**: `partial:Q9 — makes the substitution and asserts resolvability without naming an owner or mechanism; partial:RD-7 — the reservation is recorded as satisfied by a digest`
- **Rationale**: The secrets hazard the swap dissolves is real, but a bounded alternative exists and is not considered (ship the non-secret numeric columns — the bucket budgets — and digest the rest). The report chose all-or-nothing and asserted the missing half is available elsewhere.

#### cross-cutting-24 — Q12 RD-2/RD-3 clocks / environment

- **Mode** *(medium)*: Whether `wallMs` is sampled per record or derived from the origin is unruled, and a RAM-disk device is the case where that choice bites.
- **Mechanism**: RD-2 stores "wall epoch-ms and a monotonic offset from one shared origin" per record and per cut marker, with "deltas derived at replay, never stored". Nothing says whether `wallMs` is `Date.now()` at capture or `clockOrigin.wallMs + monoMs`. A RAM-disk device boots with no persisted clock state and steps its wall clock when NTP syncs — during or just after the boot window that Q7's pre-arm stub exists to cover. If `wallMs` is sampled, records go backwards in wall time within a bucket while `seq` and `monoMs` stay monotonic; if derived, the header's wall origin is simply wrong for everything after the step and no field records that a step occurred.
- **Effect**: Cross-bucket interleaving reconstructed by wall time disagrees with per-bucket `seq` order, and correlating a bundle against server-side logs (the whole point of a wall clock) fails by the size of the step — silently, since nothing detects or marks it.
- **Owner**: `partial:Q12 — reserves two clocks and one origin without ruling the sampling rule or the step case`
- **Rationale**: RD-3's justification ("a single-threaded runtime needs no vector clock") answers concurrency, not clock discontinuity. The design has the fields to detect a step (a per-record pair) and no rule that uses them.

#### cross-cutting-25 — Q7 pre-arm stub

- **Mode** *(medium)*: Pre-boot errors replayed from the stub have no clock domain and no sequence domain of their own, so they are timestamped at replay.
- **Mechanism**: Q7's stub is "~30 owned lines that register `error`/`unhandledrejection` listeners into a bounded array... which the recorder drains and replays into its own nets at init". `clockOrigin` is minted at recorder construction, after the stub has run; `seq` is ring-assigned at push (RD-1). Nothing specifies that the stub captures timestamps, and if it did, they would predate the origin the header declares.
- **Effect**: The boot-window failures the stub exists to capture arrive in the bundle stamped with post-boot times, collapsing the pre-boot interval to a point at init — which destroys the ordering evidence (what failed before what) that a boot-loop diagnosis needs, in the one error class the stub was added for.
- **Owner**: `partial:Q7 — the stub's existence and queue-and-replay pattern are ruled; its clock, ordering and identity semantics are not`
- **Rationale**: Q7 treats the stub as a capture-coverage fix. Nothing connects it to Q12's clock reservations or to the header's single `clockOrigin`, and the spike list defers the stub to "0120's future harness", so the gap would not surface before integration.

#### cross-cutting-26 — Q6 attach/supersede semantics / bundle evidence
