- **Mode** *(medium)*: A sealed bucket carries one attach epoch for a record array that may span several, so a source discontinuity is invisible in the record stream.
- **Mechanism**: `attach()` increments `attachEpoch` on supersede, `detach()` never discards the ring ("the ring, its records, and the death marker remain for the next bundle"), and the worked example explicitly recreates the boundary and re-attaches. `SealedBucket.attach` carries a single `epoch: number` and `CapturedRecord` carries no epoch field. `attachedSince`/`detachedAt`/`lastRecordAt` are bare `number`s while every record carries a `{wallMs, monoMs}` pair, so the death cannot even be located in the records' clock domain reliably.
- **Effect**: After a boundary death and recreation, one mqtt bucket presents a continuous `seq`-ordered stream spanning two broker sessions — with a gap, a possible replay burst under `clean: false`, and different subscription state on either side — and the bundle offers no marker at the discontinuity's position in the stream. A reader reconstructs a history that never happened, with all counters honest.
- **Owner**: `partial:Q6 — the panel adopted "detach never discards the ring — when capture stopped is itself evidence" and gave the bundle one epoch stamp for a multi-epoch ring; partial:Q12 — RD-9's `oldestIsComplete` covers eviction, not source change`
- **Rationale**: The interface has everything needed (epoch, both clocks, per-record fields) and simply does not stamp epoch per record or write a boundary marker into the ring at detach. The plan's own MQTT reservation calls for "explicit gap markers in arrival order", which this path does not produce.

#### cross-cutting-27 — Q1 the recorder's distinct claim / Q13 intake item b

- **Mode** *(medium)*: A plausible answer to a gating intake item voids the design's entire value with no fallback, because the panel refused any way to read the rings locally.
- **Mechanism**: Q13's declared assumption until intake b answers is structural-skeleton redaction, and item b may answer more restrictively still (nothing leaves the device). Q6's panel records among "the judge's highest-value rejections... any read/query API over ring contents (the only reader is the seal)", and `FlightRecorder` exposes exactly three entry points, none of which returns records. `capture()` returns a receipt; the bundle goes only to `transport`.
- **Effect**: If payloads may not leave the device, the recorder retains a full-fidelity history that no human can reach — no devtools dump, no local export, no on-device diagnosis path — and Q1's unique claim ("retrospective, full-fidelity, cross-bucket context") reduces to shapes and counters. The skip verdict Q1 weighed would then have been the correct one, and the report has no branch that says so.
- **Owner**: `partial:Q1 — states the skip verdict's honest content against the accepted surfaces, not against a restrictive answer to item b; partial:Q13 — names item b as gating shipping without saying what it gates *to*`
- **Rationale**: Risk 2 marks item b's dependents "provisional", which is right for the redaction rules. It does not register that one answer collapses the recommendation itself, and the interface's deliberate lack of a read path removes the obvious mitigation.

#### cross-cutting-28 — Rubric — Runtime overhead row / bundle-size accounting

- **Mode** *(medium)*: The build posture's size figure counts only its two dependencies and is scored against competitors' whole-SDK figures.
- **Mechanism**: The rubric scores build "strong — ~6.5 KB gzip of deps plus the owned kit" (2,513 B + 4,027 B) against Sentry "≤33 KB gzip error-only", Bugsnag 15.1 KB and Faro 34.4 KB — all whole-package figures. The owned kit has no estimate anywhere, while the report itself enumerates ring (~60 lines), nets (~200–250 lines), pre-arm stub (~50 lines), plus the projection wrapper, redaction applier, meter, seal, bundle assembler, gzip worker (a separate chunk), delivery loop with backoff, config validator, four tap adapters and the React boundary — several thousand lines before minification.
- **Effect**: The one criterion where adoption should show an advantage is scored on incomparable units, flattering build by the entire size of the thing being built. A reader cannot check the claim, and the rubric's "strong" is not supported by any figure in the report.
- **Owner**: `partial:Rubric Runtime overhead — acknowledges "plus the owned kit" and supplies no number for it; UNOWNED for a size budget on the owned kit`
- **Rationale**: Q14's bill of materials lists what is owned but never sizes it, and no key question owns bundle budget. The fix is arithmetic, not new research: an owned-kit gzip estimate would make the row comparable.

#### cross-cutting-29 — Cross-track — 0150 durable inbox on a shared RAM-disk origin

- **Mode** *(high)*: The recorder can fail 0150's durability guarantee through memory, a channel neither track's boundary analysis covers.
- **Mechanism**: The report's separation-of-concerns note says the two tracks "meet only if question 10 adopts durable parking", and Q10 refuses parking — so the report concludes there is no interaction. But on a RAM-disk device IndexedDB is RAM (facts/app-profile.md:293-303): 0150's inbox store, its dedup keys (~1.2 MB per hour of retention) and its transactions compete for the same physical budget as the recorder's 34 MiB of rings per tab plus up to two held bundles. IndexedDB writes fail under storage pressure, and 0150's central claim is that the effect and the identity commit in one transaction.
- **Effect**: The recorder — which touches no IndexedDB and takes no transaction — can still cause 0150's inbox transaction to fail, i.e. degrade the app's durability guarantee, purely by occupying RAM. "The recorder must never degrade the app" has been verified against latency and throw containment and never against memory as a shared resource with a neighbouring system.
- **Owner**: `UNOWNED`
- **Rationale**: Q10 analyses the interaction in one direction only (the recorder adopting parking, inheriting 0150's origin/schema/eviction decisions). Q11's hazard list has no shared-resource item. The plan's "Distinct from neighbours" section asserts the tracks meet only through parking, which is true of code and false of RAM.

#### cross-cutting-30 — Process — the standing D-0036 obligation

- **Mode** *(medium)*: The report asserts the design-level enumeration obligation is discharged and links a register that does not exist.
- **Mechanism**: Q15 states "**The standing obligation is discharged beside this report**: the same D-0036-shape enumeration... ran again over this report's recommended design; its register is [design-fmea-enumeration.md](design-fmea-enumeration.md), and the amendments it forced are annotated in place in this report." The constraints paragraph repeats the claim. `ls tracks/0160-flight-recorder/` shows only design-panel-record.md, plan-fmea-enumeration.md, report.md, research-plan.md — the link is dangling, and no amendment in the report can have been forced by findings not yet produced.
- **Effect**: A gate reviewer checking the plan's question 15 obligation against the report's own text would record it as met on the strength of a broken link, and the report's "annotated in place" claim invites a reader to believe design-level findings were absorbed when none had been.
- **Owner**: `UNOWNED`
- **Rationale**: This is a defect in the report's own text of the same class as the plan-level pass's items 3 and 13 (a plan asserting two different retry nulls). It is trivially fixable once this pass lands, but as written the report claims a completed control it had not run.

## Appendix C — intake items this pass raised, pending addition

Two facts the enumeration surfaced were not written into
`intake/2026-08-22-0160-recorder-facts.md` because the file was being answered by its owner at
the time. They are recorded here so they are not lost, and the report references them as
"raised for intake, not yet lettered" rather than citing a letter that does not exist.

**Content-Security-Policy and how `index.html` is served.** Does the app ship a CSP, and does
its `script-src` permit inline scripts (`'unsafe-inline'`) or a per-response nonce? Vite 8
emits a static `index.html`, so a nonce would have to be injected by whatever serves it. The
report's boot-window compensation is an inline pre-arm stub in the document head; under a
restrictive CSP it silently never runs, and an empty replay queue at init is byte-identical to
"no pre-boot errors occurred". This survey used the same CSP lens to disqualify fast-redact as
a dependency, so the recorder's own prescribed script owes the same check. If inline is barred,
the fallback is an external stub file loaded before the app bundle.
→ Resolution: settles report question 7's pre-arm design; updates facts/app-profile.md.

**Outbound MQTT publish volume.** Roughly what share of the ~50 msg/s aggregate is outbound
publishes rather than inbound messages? The app profile records the aggregate with no
inbound/outbound split. Report question 5 declines to pay for the outbound capture gap partly
on the assumption that outbound volume is small; if it is material, the pre-authorized
policy-row emission route in 0060 should be reconsidered rather than deferred.
→ Resolution: confirms or overturns report question 5's verdict.

One addition also belongs on the existing **item c** (the report endpoint's particulars): the
last-gasp path sends a small **loss marker** — a few hundred bytes of JSON in a `text/plain`
Blob, sent by `sendBeacon` — which is a second payload shape alongside the bundle. Does the
endpoint have (or can it have) a route that accepts it? A beacon's response is unobservable,
so if the endpoint discards it, the client can never learn that the compensation for refusing
durable parking is not working.
